# List 11

## Unit 1

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>fussy</b></td><td>/ˈfʌsi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>挑剔的，过分讲究的</td><td>fussy about small details<br>对小细节很挑剔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old tailor is fussy about the smallest details.  这位老裁缝对最小的细节都很挑剔。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 careful</td><td>/ˈkerfəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>谨慎的，仔细的</td><td>careful consideration<br>慎重考虑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Be careful when you cross this busy street at night.  夜里穿过这条繁忙的街道时要小心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exact</td><td>/ɪɡˈzækt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>强索，征收；精确的</td><td>exact a heavy tax<br>征收重税</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The conquerors exacted tribute from every village in the valley.  征服者向谷中每个村庄强征贡赋。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 meticulous</td><td>/məˈtɪkjələs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>一丝不苟的，极为细致的</td><td>meticulous attention to detail<br>对细节极为在意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The restorer's meticulous attention to detail saved the fragile manuscript from further damage.  修复师一丝不苟地关注细节，使这份脆弱的手稿免于进一步损坏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 punctilious</td><td>/pʌŋkˈtɪliəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>注意细节的，一丝不苟的</td><td>punctilious about details<br>对细节一丝不苟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is punctilious about details that others simply ignore.  他对别人干脆忽略的细节一丝不苟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 choosy</td><td>/ˈtʃuːzi/</td><td>★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>挑剔的，苛求的</td><td>choosy about food<br>对食物挑剔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is so choosy about coffee that he grinds his own beans.  他对咖啡挑剔到自己磨豆子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delicate</td><td>/ˈdeləkət/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>微妙的，精细的；脆弱的</td><td>a delicate distinction<br>微妙的区别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She has a delicate ear for the subtle differences between dialects.  她对方言之间的细微差别有很灵敏的耳朵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 demanding</td><td>/dɪˈmændɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>难取悦的，难满足的；费时间花心思的</td><td>a demanding new job<br>费心费力的新工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Caring for three small children is demanding work with no holidays.  照顾三个小孩是费时间花心思且没有休假的工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exacting</td><td>/ɪɡˈzæktɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>严格的，苛求的；费时间花心思的</td><td>exacting quality standards<br>严格的质量标准</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The airline maintains exacting standards for every part it installs.  这家航空公司对安装的每个部件都保持严格的标准。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fastidious</td><td>/fæˈstɪdiəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>挑剔的，极仔细的，追求完美的</td><td>fastidious about details<br>对细节极挑剔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is fastidious about details and rereads every line twice.  她对细节极为挑剔，每一行都要读两遍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nice</td><td>/ˈnaɪs/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>规矩得体的；令人愉快的</td><td>nice manners<br>得体的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is a nice boy who never gives his parents trouble.  他是个规矩的男孩，从不给父母添麻烦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 particular</td><td>/pərˈtɪkjələr/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>详细的；特定的；讲究的</td><td>a particular account of events<br>对事件的详细说明</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gave a particular account of every repair the house needed.  他详细说明了房子所需的每一项修缮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 persnickety</td><td>/pərˈsnɪkəti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过分挑剔的</td><td>a persnickety inspector<br>过分挑剔的检查员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The persnickety inspector rejected the wall over one small crack.  这位挑剔的检查员因一条小裂缝就否决了那面墙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 picky</td><td>/ˈpɪki/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>挑剔的，难满足的</td><td>a picky eater<br>挑食的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 My nephew is a picky eater who refuses all vegetables.  我侄子很挑食，什么蔬菜都不吃。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 careless</td><td>/ˈkerləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗心的，疏忽的</td><td>a careless mistake<br>粗心的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One careless remark ruined months of patient negotiation.  一句不慎的话毁了数月的耐心谈判。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 undemanding</td><td>/ˌʌndɪˈmændɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>要求不高的，轻松的</td><td>an undemanding course<br>要求不高的课程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She chose an undemanding role so she could rest more.  她选了个轻松的角色以便多休息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unfussy</td><td>/ʌnˈfʌsi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不挑剔的，不讲究的</td><td>an unfussy eater<br>不挑食的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is an unfussy traveler who can sleep anywhere.  她是个不讲究的旅行者，在哪都能睡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>fusty</b></td><td>/ˈfʌsti/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有霉味的，霉臭的；过时的</td><td>a fusty old room<br>一间霉味的旧房间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They opened every window to air out the fusty old room.  他们打开每扇窗给这间霉味的旧房间通风。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 antiquated</td><td>/ˈæntɪkweɪtɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>古老的，过时的</td><td>an antiquated system<br>过时的系统</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The office still runs on an antiquated system built in the eighties.  办公室仍在使用八十年代建成的过时系统。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 archaic</td><td>/ɑːrˈkeɪɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>过时的，久远的</td><td>archaic legal language<br>古旧的法律用语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The contract still uses archaic terms that nobody understands.  这份合同仍在使用无人理解的古旧术语。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bygone</td><td>/ˈbaɪˌɡɔːn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过去的，已往的</td><td>a bygone era<br>逝去的年代</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mansion still recalls the elegance of a bygone age.  这座宅邸仍让人想起逝去年代的优雅。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 moldy</td><td>/ˈmoʊldi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>发霉的；陈腐的</td><td>moldy bread<br>发霉的面包</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cellar smelled of moldy paper and damp stone.  地窖里散发着霉纸和潮湿石头的气味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 outdated</td><td>/ˈaʊtˌdeɪtɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过时的，陈旧的</td><td>outdated equipment<br>过时的设备</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their outdated software could not handle the new files.  他们过时的软件无法处理新文件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 malodorous</td><td>/ˌmælˈoʊdərəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶臭的</td><td>malodorous waste<br>恶臭的废弃物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Inspectors traced the malodorous discharge to a nearby factory.  检查人员追查到那股恶臭排放来自附近一家工厂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fetid</td><td>/ˈfetɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶臭的</td><td>fetid air<br>恶臭的空气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A fetid smell rose from the blocked drain behind the house.  屋后堵塞的下水道散出恶臭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 musty</td><td>/ˈmʌsti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>发霉的，霉味的；陈腐的</td><td>a musty smell<br>一股霉味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cellar was dark, damp, and full of musty old books.  地窖阴暗潮湿，堆满带霉味的旧书。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 noisome</td><td>/ˈnɔɪsəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>恶臭的；令人极厌恶的</td><td>a noisome odor<br>一股恶臭气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Workers refused to enter the noisome tunnel without masks.  工人们不戴口罩不肯进入那条恶臭的隧道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 smelly</td><td>/ˈsmeli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>难闻的，有臭味的</td><td>smelly socks<br>有臭味的袜子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bin in the corner was getting distinctly smelly.  角落里的垃圾桶明显开始发臭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stale</td><td>/steɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈旧乏味的</td><td>stale jokes<br>老掉牙的笑话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His stale jokes made the audience shift restlessly in their seats.  他老掉牙的笑话让观众在座位上不安地挪动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ambrosial</td><td>/æmˈbroʊziəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>特别美味的；芬芳的</td><td>an ambrosial dessert<br>美味无比的甜点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The soup had an ambrosial aroma that filled the whole house.  这汤散发的美妙香气弥漫了整座房子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 aromatic</td><td>/ˌerəˈmætɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>芳香的</td><td>aromatic herbs<br>芳香的草药</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The kitchen was full of aromatic spices from the market.  厨房里满是从市场买来的芳香香料。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fragrant</td><td>/ˈfreɪɡrənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>芳香的，香的</td><td>fragrant flowers<br>芳香的花朵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The kitchen was fragrant with fresh bread and cinnamon.  厨房里弥漫着新鲜面包和肉桂的香气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 perfumed</td><td>/pərˈfjuːmd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有香味的，洒了香水的</td><td>perfumed soap<br>香皂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She wrote the letter on thin perfumed paper.  她用薄薄的香纸写了那封信。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 redolent</td><td>/ˈredələnt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>芳香的；令人想起…的</td><td>redolent of spices<br>散发香料气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old room was redolent of pipe smoke and leather.  那间旧屋弥漫着烟斗烟味和皮革气息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 savory</td><td>/ˈseɪvəri/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>美味的，可口的</td><td>a savory dish<br>一道可口的菜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She served a savory pie made with mushrooms and herbs.  她端上一道用蘑菇和香草做的美味派。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 scented</td><td>/ˈsentɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有香味的，加香的</td><td>scented candles<br>香味蜡烛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She wrote all her letters on pale scented paper.  她所有的信都写在淡色的香味纸上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sweet</td><td>/ˈswiːt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>甜的；温和的，亲切的</td><td>in a sweet tone<br>以温和的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spoke in a sweet voice that calmed the frightened child.  她用温柔的声音说话，安抚了害怕的孩子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>futile</b></td><td>/ˈfjuːtl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无效的，无用的</td><td>a futile attempt<br>徒劳的尝试</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Trying to persuade him was clearly a futile attempt.  试图说服他显然是徒劳之举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abortive</td><td>/əˈbɔːrtɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>失败的，无结果的</td><td>an abortive coup<br>一次失败的政变</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After two abortive attempts, they abandoned the climb.  两次尝试失败后，他们放弃了攀登。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 bootless</del></td><td>/ˈbuːtləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无用的</td><td>a bootless effort<br>无用的努力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Arguing with him about money proved a bootless effort.  和他争论钱的事结果是无用的努力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fruitless</td><td>/ˈfruːtləs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无成效的，白费的</td><td>a fruitless search<br>毫无结果的搜寻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Weeks of fruitless negotiation left both sides exhausted.  数周毫无结果的谈判让双方都疲惫不堪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ineffective</td><td>/ˌɪnɪˈfektɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无效的，不起作用的</td><td>an ineffective treatment<br>无效的治疗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old medicine proved completely ineffective against the new virus.  这种旧药对新病毒完全无效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 useless</td><td>/ˈjuːsləs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无用的，无效的</td><td>practically useless<br>几乎毫无用处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Without a charger the expensive phone was completely useless.  没有充电器，这部贵手机完全没用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vain</td><td>/ˈveɪn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>徒劳的；自负的</td><td>in vain<br>徒劳地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They searched all night in vain for the missing dog.  他们整夜寻找失踪的狗，却是徒劳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 effectual</td><td>/ɪˈfɛktʃuəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有效的，起作用的</td><td>an effectual remedy<br>有效的疗法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Simple hand washing proved the most effectual precaution available.  简单的洗手被证明是最有效的预防措施。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 efficacious</td><td>/ˌefɪˈkeɪʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有效的</td><td>an efficacious treatment<br>有效的治疗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old remedy proved surprisingly efficacious against mild seasonal coughs.  这个老方子治轻微的季节性咳嗽出乎意料地有效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>gadfly</b></td><td>/ˈɡædflaɪ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>（爱挑刺的）刺激者；牛虻</td><td>a political gadfly<br>政治上的刺激者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The columnist served as a gadfly to a complacent government.  这位专栏作家是自满政府的刺激者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 goad</td><td>/ɡoʊd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>刺激，驱使，激发</td><td>goad him into action<br>激他采取行动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His teammates goaded him into accepting the ridiculous bet.  队友们激他接受了那个荒唐的赌约。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impetus</td><td>/ˈɪmpətəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>推动力，促进因素</td><td>give fresh impetus to<br>为……提供新动力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The grant gave fresh impetus to their stalled research.  这笔资助为他们停滞的研究提供了新动力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impulse</td><td>/ˈɪmpəls/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>冲动；推动力</td><td>act on impulse<br>凭一时冲动行事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She bought the painting on impulse and never regretted it.  她一时冲动买下那幅画，从未后悔。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 incentive</td><td>/ɪnˈsentɪv/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>刺激，诱因</td><td>a tax incentive<br>税收上的激励</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Tax breaks gave farmers a strong incentive to plant native trees.  税收减免给了农民种植本土树木的强烈诱因。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 irritant</td><td>/ˈɪrɪtənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>刺激物；恼人的事物</td><td>a constant irritant<br>持续的烦扰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cigarette smoke is a serious irritant to sensitive eyes.  香烟烟雾对敏感的眼睛是严重的刺激物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 spur</td><td>/spɜːr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>刺激物</td><td>a spur to growth<br>增长的刺激因素</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rising exports acted as a powerful spur to industrial growth.  出口增长成为工业发展的强大刺激因素。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stimulus</td><td>/ˈstɪmjələs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>刺激物，促进因素</td><td>an economic stimulus<br>经济刺激措施</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cheap loans acted as a stimulus to small businesses.  低息贷款成为小企业的刺激因素。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 balm</td><td>/bɑːm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>香油，止痛膏；安慰物</td><td>a balm for grief<br>抚慰悲伤之物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her quiet letters were a balm for his grief that winter.  那年冬天，她安静的信件是抚慰他悲伤之物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>gaffe</b></td><td>/ɡæf/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>（社交上）失礼，失态</td><td>a diplomatic gaffe<br>外交失礼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His remark about the queen was a serious diplomatic gaffe.  他关于女王的言论是严重的外交失礼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impropriety</td><td>/ˌɪmprəˈpraɪəti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不得体，失礼；不当行为</td><td>financial impropriety<br>财务上的不当行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He resigned amid allegations of financial impropriety.  他在财务不当行为的指控中辞职。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 indecorum</del></td><td>/ˌɪndɪˈkɔrəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>失礼，不得体</td><td>a moment of indecorum<br>一时的失礼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her single moment of indecorum was discussed for years.  她一时的失礼被议论了好几年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 indiscretion</td><td>/ˌɪndɪsˈkreʃən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>不慎的言行，轻率</td><td>a youthful indiscretion<br>年轻时的轻率之举</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One indiscretion in an interview cost him the appointment.  访谈中的一次失言让他失去了这个职位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 decency</td><td>/ˈdiːsənsi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>得体，体面；正派</td><td>common decency<br>基本的体面礼数</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He did not even have the decency to apologize.  他连道歉的基本礼数都没有。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 decorum</td><td>/dɪˈkɔːrəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>礼貌得体，端庄</td><td>behave with proper decorum<br>举止得体有礼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Guests were asked to observe decorum during the memorial service.  来宾被要求在追悼会上保持得体举止。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 propriety</td><td>/prəˈpraɪəti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>适当，得体</td><td>a sense of propriety<br>得体的分寸感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her sense of propriety kept her from raising the subject at dinner.  她的得体分寸感使她没有在晚餐时提起这个话题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>gainsay</b></td><td>/ˌɡeɪnˈseɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>否认</td><td>gainsay the evidence<br>否认证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No one could gainsay the evidence the inspector had collected.  没有人能否认检查员收集到的证据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deny</td><td>/dɪˈnaɪ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>否认；拒绝给予</td><td>flatly deny the charge<br>断然否认指控</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I do not deny that the plan carries some very real risks.  我不否认这个计划确实带有一些实实在在的风险。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 contradict</td><td>/ˌkɑːntrəˈdɪkt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>否认，反驳；与…相矛盾</td><td>contradict the evidence<br>与证据相矛盾</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness's second statement flatly contradicted everything she had said earlier.  证人的第二份陈述与她此前所说完全矛盾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 contravene</td><td>/ˌkɑːntrəˈviːn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>违反，反对</td><td>contravene the rules<br>违反规定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Building a factory here would contravene several local environmental regulations.  在此建厂会违反数条当地环保法规。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 naysay</del></td><td>/ˈneɪseɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>拒绝，否认</td><td>naysay the proposal<br>否认这项提议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Board members naysay every proposal that requires spending money on new equipment.  董事会成员否决每一项需要花钱买新设备的提议。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 acknowledge</td><td>/ækˈnɑːlɪdʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>承认；对…表示感谢</td><td>acknowledge the mistake<br>承认错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor finally acknowledged that the report contained serious errors.  市长最终承认那份报告有严重错误。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 admit</td><td>/ədˈmɪt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>承认；准许进入</td><td>admit the mistake<br>承认错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She finally admitted that she had made a serious mistake.  她终于承认自己犯了一个严重的错误。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 avow</td><td>/əˈvaʊ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>公开承认，坦率声明</td><td>avow one's beliefs openly<br>公开表明信念</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He avowed his support for the strike in front of reporters.  他在记者面前公开表明支持这次罢工。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 concede</td><td>/kənˈsiːd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>承认</td><td>concede the point<br>承认这一点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She finally conceded that her opponent had made a fair point.  她最终承认对手说得有道理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>gall</b></td><td>/ɡɔːl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（使）焦躁，激怒</td><td>galled by the remark<br>被那句话惹恼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His smug tone galled everyone sitting around the conference table.  他自鸣得意的语气激怒了围坐在会议桌旁的每个人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 aggravate</td><td>/ˈæɡrəveɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>加重，恶化；激怒</td><td>aggravate the situation<br>使局势恶化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Running on the injury will only aggravate it.  带着伤跑步只会让伤势加重。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exasperate</td><td>/ɪɡˈzæspəreɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使恼怒，激怒</td><td>exasperate his parents<br>使他的父母恼火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His constant lateness exasperated even the most patient teachers.  他总是迟到，连最有耐心的老师都被激怒了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grate</td><td>/ɡreɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>刮擦（以发出刺耳的声音）；骚扰，惹恼</td><td>grate on my nerves<br>让我心烦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her constant humming began to grate on everyone in the small office.  她不停的哼唱开始让小办公室里的每个人心烦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inflame</td><td>/ɪnˈfleɪm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使加剧</td><td>inflame public anger<br>使公众愤怒加剧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor's careless comments only inflamed public anger over the housing shortage.  市长轻率的言论只是加剧了公众对住房短缺的愤怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 provoke</td><td>/prəˈvoʊk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激怒；驱使，激起</td><td>provoke an angry response<br>引发愤怒的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His careless remark provoked an angry response from the whole audience.  他不慎的言论激起了全体观众的愤怒回应。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pique</td><td>/piːk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激起（兴趣）；使生气</td><td>pique her curiosity<br>激起她的好奇心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The strange title piqued her curiosity, so she borrowed the book.  奇怪的书名激起了她的好奇心，于是她借走了那本书。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 roil</td><td>/rɔɪl/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激怒</td><td>roil the crowd<br>激怒人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The careless remarks of the mayor roiled the crowd outside the courthouse.  市长轻率的言论激怒了法院外的人群。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 appease</td><td>/əˈpiːz/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>安抚，平息；满足</td><td>appease the angry crowd<br>安抚愤怒的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company offered refunds to appease its angry customers.  公司提供退款以安抚愤怒的顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 assuage</td><td>/əˈsweɪdʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>缓和，减轻；平息</td><td>assuage his fears<br>减轻他的恐惧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor's speech did little to assuage public anger.  市长的演讲几乎没能平息公众的怒火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 calm</td><td>/ˈkɑːm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、动词、名词</td><td>平静的；使镇静</td><td>calm down quickly<br>很快平静下来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spoke in a calm voice until the children settled.  她用平静的声音说话，直到孩子们安静下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lull</td><td>/lʌl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>相对平静时期，间隙；使镇静，使安心</td><td>a lull in the fighting<br>战斗中的间隙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A brief lull in the fighting allowed medics to reach the wounded.  战斗中短暂的间隙让医护人员得以接近伤员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pacify</td><td>/ˈpæsɪfaɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使平静，安慰</td><td>pacify a crying baby<br>安抚哭闹的婴儿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nurse sang softly to pacify the frightened child before the injection.  打针前，护士轻声唱歌安抚受惊的孩子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 placate</td><td>/ˈpleɪkeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（通过让步以）平息抚慰</td><td>placate the critics<br>平息批评者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company offered full refunds to placate its angriest customers.  公司提供全额退款以平息最愤怒的客户。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>gallant</b></td><td>/ˈɡælənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>英勇的</td><td>a gallant defense<br>英勇的防守</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The gallant paratroopers jumped from the plane without a moment's hesitation.  英勇的伞兵们毫不犹豫地跳出了飞机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bold</td><td>/ˈboʊld/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>大胆的，勇敢的；醒目的</td><td>a bold move<br>大胆的举动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was a bold decision to leave a secure job.  辞去稳定的工作是个大胆的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 courageous</td><td>/kəˈreɪdʒəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>勇敢的，有胆量的</td><td>a courageous decision<br>勇敢的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was courageous of him to speak out against the policy.  他公开反对这项政策，很有勇气。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dauntless</td><td>/ˈdɔntləs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无畏的，勇敢的</td><td>dauntless courage<br>无畏的勇气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dauntless nurse worked through shelling to reach the wounded.  这位无畏的护士在炮击中赶去救伤员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 heroic</td><td>/hɪˈroʊɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>英勇的，英雄般的</td><td>a heroic rescue<br>英勇的营救</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Firefighters made a heroic effort to save the trapped children.  消防员为营救被困儿童做出了英勇的努力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 stouthearted</del></td><td>/ˌstaʊtˈhɑːrtɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>勇敢的</td><td>stouthearted defenders<br>勇敢的守卫者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A handful of stouthearted defenders held the bridge until dawn.  少数勇敢的守卫者守住桥梁直到黎明。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 valorous</td><td>/ˈvælərəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>勇猛的，英勇的</td><td>a valorous knight<br>英勇的骑士</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Songs still honor the valorous soldiers who held the bridge.  歌曲仍在颂扬守住那座桥的英勇士兵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 craven</td><td>/ˈkreɪvn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非常懦弱的，因胆小而遭人鄙视的</td><td>a craven retreat<br>怯懦的撤退</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His craven silence during the trial disappointed everyone who had trusted him.  他在庭审中懦弱的沉默让所有信任过他的人失望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pusillanimous</td><td>/ˌpjuːsɪˈlænɪməs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>胆小的，懦弱的</td><td>a pusillanimous response<br>软弱的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics called the government's pusillanimous silence a betrayal of victims.  批评者称政府懦弱的沉默是对受害者的背叛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>galvanize</b></td><td>/ˈɡælvənaɪz/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（好似用电击）刺激</td><td>galvanize the team into action<br>激励团队行动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach's angry speech galvanized the team into immediate action.  教练愤怒的讲话激励球队立刻行动起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 provoke</td><td>/prəˈvoʊk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激怒；驱使，激起</td><td>provoke an angry response<br>引发愤怒的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His careless remark provoked an angry response from the whole audience.  他不慎的言论激起了全体观众的愤怒回应。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 agitate</td><td>/ˈædʒɪteɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>煽动，激起；使不安</td><td>agitate for reform<br>鼓动改革</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Students agitated for changes to the exam system.  学生们为改革考试制度而奔走呼吁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 excite</td><td>/ɪkˈsaɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激起，引发；使兴奋</td><td>excite public interest<br>激起公众兴趣</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His speech excited strong resentment among the workers.  他的演讲激起了工人们的强烈不满。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 intoxicate</td><td>/ɪnˈtɑːksɪkeɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使陶醉；使醉</td><td>intoxicate her with praise<br>用赞美使她陶醉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sudden success seemed to intoxicate the young writer completely.  突来的成功似乎让这位年轻作家彻底陶醉了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 motivate</td><td>/ˈmoʊtɪveɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>刺激，激发</td><td>motivate the students<br>激励学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Small weekly rewards motivate the students far better than long lectures do.  每周的小奖励比长篇说教更能激励学生。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stimulate</td><td>/ˈstɪmjəˌleɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>刺激，激发；促进</td><td>stimulate economic growth<br>促进经济增长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Warm rain stimulated the seeds to sprout within a week.  温暖的雨水刺激种子一周内发芽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pump up</td><td>/pʌmp ˈʌp/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>使增加，抬高；给…充气</td><td>pump up the price<br>抬高价格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Advertising pumped up demand until the small factory could not cope.  广告抬高需求，直到小工厂应付不来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 allay</td><td>/əˈleɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>减轻，平息</td><td>allay public fears<br>平息公众恐慌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The statement was meant to allay concerns about safety.  这份声明意在减轻人们对安全的担忧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lull</td><td>/lʌl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>相对平静时期，间隙；使镇静，使安心</td><td>a lull in the fighting<br>战斗中的间隙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A brief lull in the fighting allowed medics to reach the wounded.  战斗中短暂的间隙让医护人员得以接近伤员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pacify</td><td>/ˈpæsɪfaɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使平静，安慰</td><td>pacify a crying baby<br>安抚哭闹的婴儿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nurse sang softly to pacify the frightened child before the injection.  打针前，护士轻声唱歌安抚受惊的孩子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>gamble</b></td><td>/ˈɡæmbl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>赌博；投机；冒险</td><td>gamble on the outcome<br>押注于结果</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They gambled on the outcome of the election and lost everything.  他们押注选举结果，结果输光了一切。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bet</td><td>/ˈbet/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>打赌，押注；赌注</td><td>place a bet<br>下赌注</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He bet fifty dollars on the horse that eventually won.  他在最终夺冠的那匹马上押了五十美元。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 adventure</td><td>/ədˈventʃər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>冒险；冒险活动</td><td>a risky adventure<br>危险的冒险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They set off on an adventure across the empty desert.  他们出发穿越空旷的沙漠去冒险。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 chance</td><td>/ˈtʃæns/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>冒险；机会，可能性</td><td>take a chance<br>冒险一试</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She decided to take a chance and invest all her savings.  她决定冒险一试，投入全部积蓄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 risk</td><td>/ˈrɪsk/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>风险；冒…的危险</td><td>take a big risk<br>承担很大风险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He risked his savings on a completely new business.  他拿积蓄冒险投入一项全新的生意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stake</td><td>/ˈsteɪk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>赌注；押上，以…打赌</td><td>raise the stakes<br>提高赌注</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They staked their entire fortune on that single deal.  他们把全部财产押在那一笔交易上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 venture</td><td>/ˈventʃər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>冒险；冒险事业</td><td>a risky venture<br>一项冒险的事业</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He ventured his whole savings on an untested new product.  他把全部积蓄冒险投在一款未经检验的新产品上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 2

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>gambol</b></td><td>/ˈɡæmbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>欢跳，雀跃</td><td>gambol in the meadow<br>在草地上欢跳</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Young lambs gamboled in the meadow all through the warm afternoon.  小羊羔整个温暖的下午都在草地上欢跳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frolic</td><td>/ˈfrɑːlɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>嬉戏，欢闹</td><td>frolic in the snow<br>在雪中嬉戏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The puppies frolicked in the garden all afternoon.  小狗们整个下午都在花园里嬉戏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 caper</td><td>/ˈkeɪpər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>欢跳，雀跃；恶作剧</td><td>caper about the room<br>在屋里欢跳</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children capered about the yard in sheer delight.  孩子们无比欢喜地在院子里雀跃。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cavort</td><td>/kəˈvɔːrt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>欢腾嬉戏，雀跃</td><td>cavort on the beach<br>在海滩上嬉戏</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The puppies cavorted happily in the fresh morning snow.  小狗们在清晨的新雪中欢快地嬉戏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 plod</td><td>/ˈplɑːd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>沉重缓慢地走，苦干</td><td>plod through the mud<br>在泥地里沉重跋涉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The tired horses plodded through the deep mud.  疲惫的马匹在深泥中沉重跋涉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 trudge</td><td>/trʌdʒ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>吃力而笨拙地走</td><td>trudge through the snow<br>在雪中吃力行走</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We trudged home through deep snow with our heavy canvas bags.  我们背着沉重的帆布包，在深雪中吃力地走回家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>gag</b></td><td>/ɡæɡ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>笑话；玩笑之举</td><td>a running gag<br>反复出现的笑料</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The same running gag appears in every episode of the series.  同一个反复出现的笑料出现在这部剧的每一集里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crack</td><td>/ˈkræk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>破解，破译</td><td>crack the enemy's code<br>破解敌方密码</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Mathematicians finally cracked the enemy's code after months of work.  数学家们经过数月努力终于破解了敌方密码。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 drollery</del></td><td>/ˈdroʊləri/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>滑稽言行，诙谐</td><td>a bit of drollery<br>一点诙谐之语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His lecture was full of gentle drollery that charmed the audience.  他的讲座充满温和的诙谐，迷住了听众。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 giggle</td><td>/ˈɡɪɡəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>咯咯地笑，傻笑</td><td>giggle nervously<br>紧张地傻笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The girls giggled at the teacher's silly joke about cats.  女孩们被老师关于猫的傻笑话逗得咯咯笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jape</td><td>/dʒeɪp/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>嘲弄</td><td>a harmless jape<br>无恶意的玩笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He japed about his own baldness to put the guests at ease.  他拿自己的秃头开玩笑，让客人们放松下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jest</td><td>/dʒest/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>玩笑，戏谑</td><td>in jest<br>开玩笑地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He said it in jest, but nobody in the room was laughing.  他是开玩笑说的，但屋里没有一个人笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 quip</td><td>/ˈkwɪp/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>妙语，俏皮话；说妙语</td><td>a witty quip<br>机智的俏皮话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He answered the hostile question with a quip that eased everyone.  他用一句俏皮话回应了敌意的提问，缓和了气氛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sally</td><td>/ˈsæli/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>妙语，机智的话；突袭</td><td>a witty sally<br>机智的妙语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her clever sally made everyone at the dinner table laugh.  她机智的妙语让餐桌上所有人都笑了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 waggery</del></td><td>/ˈwæɡəri/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>滑稽，诙谐言行</td><td>harmless waggery<br>无伤大雅的诙谐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old man's waggery kept the whole family entertained all evening.  老人的诙谐让全家整晚都很开心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wisecrack</td><td>/ˈwaɪzˌkræk/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>俏皮话，妙语</td><td>make a wisecrack<br>说句俏皮话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He made a wisecrack about the manager's new haircut.  他就经理的新发型说了句俏皮话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 witticism</td><td>/ˈwɪtɪsɪzəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>妙语，诙谐语</td><td>a sharp witticism<br>犀利的妙语</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The essay sparkles with witticisms about modern city life.  这篇文章充满关于现代都市生活的妙语。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>garble</b></td><td>/ˈɡɑːrbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>曲解，篡改，混淆（以至使无法理解）</td><td>garble the message<br>曲解这条消息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A bad connection garbled the message beyond all understanding.  信号不好把这条消息混淆得完全无法理解。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 misrepresent</td><td>/ˌmɪsˌreprɪˈzent/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>误传，篡改</td><td>misrepresent the facts<br>歪曲事实</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics accused the report of misrepresenting the facts to favor one side.  批评者指责这份报告歪曲事实以偏向一方。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 belie</td><td>/bɪˈlaɪ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>与…相对立；与…相矛盾</td><td>belie his claims<br>与他的说法矛盾</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company's own records belie his claims about the accident.  公司自己的记录与他关于事故的说法相矛盾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 color</td><td>/ˈkʌlər/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>渲染，影响（叙述）；着色</td><td>color the account<br>渲染这段叙述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His prejudice clearly colored the report he submitted to us.  他的偏见明显影响了他交给我们的报告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 distort</td><td>/dɪˈstɔːrt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>扭曲，歪曲</td><td>distort the facts<br>歪曲事实</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Editing the tape that way would distort the facts of the interview.  那样剪辑录音会歪曲采访的事实。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 falsify</td><td>/ˈfɔːlsəˌfaɪ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>篡改，伪造；证明为假</td><td>falsify the records<br>篡改记录</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An accountant was accused of falsifying the company's records.  一名会计被指控篡改公司账目。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 twist</td><td>/ˈtwɪst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>曲解，扭曲；拧</td><td>twist someone's words<br>曲解某人的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not twist my words to suit your own argument.  不要为了你自己的论点曲解我的话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 warp</td><td>/wɔːrp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>曲解</td><td>warp the truth<br>曲解真相</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A careless English translation had warped the author's original meaning.  一个粗心的英文译本曲解了作者的原意。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 clarify</td><td>/ˈklerɪfaɪ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>阐明，澄清</td><td>clarify the rules<br>澄清规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dean clarified the rules after weeks of confused complaints.  在数周混乱的投诉后，院长澄清了规则。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 elucidate</td><td>/iˈluːsɪdeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>阐明</td><td>elucidate the problem<br>阐明问题</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The footnotes elucidate the problem far better than the chapter itself does.  脚注比正文本身更好地阐明了这个问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>gargantuan</b></td><td>/ɡɑːrˈɡæntʃuən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的</td><td>a gargantuan appetite<br>巨大的食量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young athlete has a gargantuan appetite after every practice.  这名年轻运动员每次训练后都有巨大的食量。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 huge</td><td>/ˈhjuːdʒ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的，庞大的</td><td>a huge success<br>巨大的成功</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A huge crowd waited outside the stadium in the rain.  一大群人在雨中等在体育场外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 astronomical</td><td>/ˌæstrəˈnɑːmɪkəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>极巨大的；天文的</td><td>astronomical costs<br>极其高昂的费用</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The repairs came at an astronomical cost to the city.  这些维修给市政带来了天价开支。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 colossal</td><td>/kəˈlɑːsl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的</td><td>a colossal statue<br>巨大的雕像</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A colossal statue of the founder stands in the square.  广场上矗立着创始人的巨大雕像。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 elephantine</td><td>/ˌelɪˈfæntiːn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的；笨拙的</td><td>elephantine movements<br>笨重的动作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His elephantine movements on stage made the delicate dance look almost comic.  他在台上笨重的动作让这支细腻的舞蹈显得近乎滑稽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enormous</td><td>/ɪˈnɔːrməs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的，庞大的</td><td>an enormous amount of data<br>庞大的数据量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The project required an enormous amount of money and patience.  这个项目需要巨额资金和极大耐心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gigantic</td><td>/dʒaɪˈɡæntɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的</td><td>a gigantic stone statue<br>巨大的石像</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A gigantic stone statue guards the entrance to the temple.  一座巨大的石像守卫着神庙的入口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 immense</td><td>/ɪˈmens/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的，极大的</td><td>an immense fortune<br>巨额财富</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The project demanded an immense amount of time and money.  这个项目需要投入巨大的时间和金钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mammoth</td><td>/ˈmæməθ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>巨大的；猛犸象</td><td>a mammoth task<br>艰巨的任务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rebuilding the port after the storm was a mammoth undertaking.  风暴后重建港口是一项巨大的工程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 monstrous</td><td>/ˈmɑːnstrəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的；骇人的</td><td>a monstrous wave<br>巨浪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A monstrous wave rose above the tiny fishing boat.  一个巨浪在小渔船上方涌起。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 titanic</td><td>/taɪˈtænɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的，力量强大的</td><td>a titanic struggle<br>激烈无比的斗争</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two armies fought a titanic battle across the frozen river.  两支军队在冰封的河上展开了一场惊天动地的大战。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 infinitesimal</td><td>/ˌɪnfɪnɪˈtesɪməl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>极微小的，无穷小的</td><td>an infinitesimal chance<br>微乎其微的机会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The risk of infection is infinitesimal if the tools are sterile.  只要器械无菌，感染风险微乎其微。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 minuscule</td><td>/ˈmɪnəskjuːl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极小的，微不足道的</td><td>a minuscule amount<br>极小的量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A minuscule crack in the pipe caused the entire basement to flood overnight.  管道上一道极小的裂缝一夜之间让整个地下室被淹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>garish</b></td><td>/ˈɡerɪʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过于鲜艳的，过于张扬的</td><td>garish colors<br>过于鲜艳的颜色</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lobby was painted in garish colors that hurt the eyes.  大堂被漆成刺眼的过于鲜艳的颜色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gaudy</td><td>/ˈɡɔːdi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>俗丽的</td><td>gaudy jewelry<br>俗丽的首饰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She wore gaudy jewelry that clashed with her simple dress.  她戴着与朴素连衣裙不搭的俗丽首饰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blatant</td><td>/ˈbleɪtnt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明显的，公然的</td><td>a blatant lie<br>赤裸裸的谎言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report exposed a blatant disregard for safety rules.  报告揭露了对安全规则的公然无视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brazen</td><td>/ˈbreɪzn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>蛮横大胆的，厚颜无耻的；大胆自信地去面对或从事</td><td>a brazen lie<br>无耻的谎言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She told a brazen lie without even lowering her eyes.  她说了个无耻的谎言，连眼睛都没垂下。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flamboyant</td><td>/flæmˈbɔɪənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>艳丽夺目的，炫耀的，充满装饰的</td><td>a flamboyant style<br>浮夸炫目的风格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The singer wore a flamboyant coat covered in mirrors and feathers.  歌手穿着一件缀满镜片和羽毛的艳丽夺目的外套。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 glaring</td><td>/ˈɡlerɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明显的，显眼的；耀眼的</td><td>a glaring error<br>明显的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is a glaring error on the very first printed page.  印刷的第一页上就有一个明显错误。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ostentatious</td><td>/ˌɑːstenˈteɪʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>炫耀卖弄的</td><td>ostentatious display of wealth<br>炫富的排场</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His ostentatious diamond watch drew stares from everyone in the modest room.  他炫耀的钻石表引来了朴素房间里所有人的注视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dim</td><td>/ˈdɪm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>暗淡的，模糊的</td><td>a dim light<br>昏暗的灯光</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The room was too dim for reading anything but large print.  房间太暗，只能看大字。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gloomy</td><td>/ˈɡluːmi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>忧郁的</td><td>a gloomy mood<br>忧郁的心情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She grew gloomy whenever the winter rain lasted more than a week.  每当冬雨连下一周以上，她就变得忧郁起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 murky</td><td>/ˈmɜːrki/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>昏暗的，阴暗的；晦涩不清的</td><td>a murky, windowless basement<br>昏暗无窗的地下室</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The murky basement was lit by one failing bulb.  昏暗的地下室里只有一只快坏的灯泡照明。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 somber</td><td>/ˈsɑːmbər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>悲伤的，不愉快的</td><td>a somber mood<br>阴郁的情绪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A somber mood settled over the town after the funeral procession passed.  送葬队伍过后，全镇陷入阴郁的气氛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>generic</b></td><td>/dʒəˈnerɪk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无差别的，普遍的</td><td>a generic term<br>统称，泛称</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Aspirin is now a generic term for that kind of drug.  阿司匹林现在成了那类药物的统称。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blanket</td><td>/ˈblæŋkət/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>覆盖以扑灭；毯子</td><td>blanket the flames<br>覆盖扑灭火焰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Firefighters blanketed the burning oil with thick white foam.  消防员用厚厚的白色泡沫覆盖扑灭了燃烧的油。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 common</td><td>/ˈkɑːmən/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>普遍的，常见的；共有的</td><td>a common mistake<br>常见的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Spelling errors are a common problem in student essays.  拼写错误是学生作文中常见的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 general</td><td>/ˈdʒenərəl/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>总体的，普遍的；笼统的</td><td>in general terms<br>笼统地讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This book gives a general introduction to ancient Greek history.  这本书对古希腊史作了总体介绍。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 universal</td><td>/ˌjuːnəˈvɜːrsəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>普遍的，全体的</td><td>universal human rights<br>普世人权</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Music seems to have a universal appeal across all cultures.  音乐似乎在所有文化中都有普遍的感染力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 concrete</td><td>/ˈkɑːŋkriːt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>具体的，实在的；明确的</td><td>concrete evidence<br>确凿的证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The detective needed concrete evidence rather than clever theories.  侦探需要确凿的证据，而不是巧妙的推论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 distinct</td><td>/dɪˈstɪŋkt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>清晰的，明显的</td><td>a distinct impression<br>清晰的印象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is a distinct difference between the two dialects.  这两种方言之间有明显差别。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 peculiar</td><td>/pəˈkjuːliər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>奇怪的；特有的</td><td>a peculiar smell<br>一股怪味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was a peculiar smell coming from the basement.  地下室传来一股奇怪的味道。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 precise</td><td>/prɪˈsaɪs/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>精确的，明确的</td><td>precise measurements<br>精确的测量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We need precise figures before we can approve the budget.  批准预算前我们需要精确的数字。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 specific</td><td>/spəˈsɪfɪk/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明确的，特定的；专门的</td><td>a specific example<br>一个具体的例子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Can you give me a specific example of the problem?  你能给我一个这个问题的具体例子吗？</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unique</td><td>/juˈniːk/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>独一无二的，独特的</td><td>a unique opportunity<br>独一无二的机会</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Each fingerprint is unique to the person who made it.  每个指纹都是其主人独有的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>garrulous</b></td><td>/ˈɡærələs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>啰嗦的，话多令人厌烦的</td><td>a garrulous neighbor<br>唠叨的邻居</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our garrulous neighbor talked for an hour about his lawn.  我们唠叨的邻居就他的草坪讲了一个小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 talkative</td><td>/ˈtɔːkətɪv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>健谈的，爱说话的</td><td>a talkative passenger<br>健谈的乘客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our talkative driver described every building along the winding coastal road.  健谈的司机介绍了蜿蜒海滨路上的每座建筑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 chatty</td><td>/ˈtʃæti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>健谈的，爱聊天的</td><td>a chatty neighbor<br>健谈的邻居</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our chatty neighbor kept us at the door for an hour.  健谈的邻居让我们在门口站了一小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 loquacious</td><td>/ləˈkweɪʃəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>话多的</td><td>a loquacious guide<br>话多的导游</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our loquacious guide talked steadily from the museum door to the exit.  我们话多的导游从博物馆门口一直讲到出口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 verbose</td><td>/vɜːrˈboʊs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冗长的，啰嗦的</td><td>a verbose explanation<br>冗长的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her verbose report could have said the same thing in two pages.  她冗长的报告本可以用两页说完同样的内容。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 voluble</td><td>/ˈvɑːljəbl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>健谈的，话多的</td><td>a voluble speaker<br>健谈的讲话者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our voluble guide talked cheerfully about every stone in the old city.  我们健谈的导游兴致勃勃地讲述古城里的每一块石头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 laconic</td><td>/ləˈkɑːnɪk/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>简洁（以至于显得粗鲁或难以理解）的</td><td>a laconic reply<br>简短生硬的回答</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His laconic reply told us nothing about what had actually happened.  他简短生硬的回答没有告诉我们实际发生了什么。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 reserved</td><td>/rɪˈzɜːrvd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>内向的，缄默的</td><td>a quiet, reserved man<br>安静内向的男子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her father was a reserved man who rarely spoke about his feelings.  她的父亲是个内向的人，很少谈论自己的感受。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 reticent</td><td>/ˈretɪsnt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>沉默不语的</td><td>reticent about his past<br>对往事讳莫如深</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was oddly reticent about the years he spent working abroad.  他对自己在国外工作的那些年出奇地沉默不语。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 taciturn</td><td>/ˈtæsɪtɜːrn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>沉默寡言的，话少的</td><td>a taciturn farmer<br>沉默寡言的农夫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The taciturn farmer answered our questions with a nod or a shrug.  沉默寡言的农夫只用点头或耸肩回答我们的问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>guardian</b></td><td>/ˈɡɑːrdiən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>守护者；捍卫者；监护人</td><td>the guardian of tradition<br>传统的守护者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 For decades she served as the guardian of the family's oldest traditions.  几十年来，她一直是这个家族最古老传统的守护者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 custodian</td><td>/kʌˈstoʊdiən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>监护人，保管人</td><td>custodian of the records<br>档案保管人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The museum serves as custodian of these ancient manuscripts.  博物馆是这些古代手稿的保管者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 defender</td><td>/dɪˈfendər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>保卫者，捍卫者</td><td>a defender of free speech<br>言论自由的捍卫者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She became a tireless defender of migrant workers' rights.  她成了外来工权益不懈的捍卫者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 protector</td><td>/prəˈtektər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>保护者；护具</td><td>protector of the weak<br>弱者的保护者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old dog was a loyal protector of the whole family.  这条老狗是全家忠诚的保护者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>gleam</b></td><td>/ɡliːm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>闪光</td><td>a gleam of hope<br>一线希望之光</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single candle gleamed in the window of the abandoned farmhouse.  一支蜡烛在废弃农舍的窗口闪着光。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flame</td><td>/ˈfleɪm/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>火焰；燃烧，发光</td><td>burst into flames<br>突然燃起大火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The candle flame flickered in the cold draft from the window.  烛焰在窗口的冷风中闪动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flash</td><td>/ˈflæʃ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>闪光；闪耀，闪现</td><td>a flash of lightning<br>一道闪电</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A light flashed briefly in the window across the street.  街对面的窗子里闪了一下光。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 glisten</td><td>/ˈɡlɪsn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>闪光</td><td>glisten with dew<br>因露水而闪亮</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The wet leaves glistened in the early morning sunlight after the storm.  暴风雨后，湿漉漉的叶子在清晨阳光下闪闪发亮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 glitter</td><td>/ˈɡlɪtər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>闪耀，闪光；闪光饰片</td><td>glitter like diamonds<br>像钻石般闪耀</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The frozen lake glittered under the pale morning sun.  结冰的湖面在淡淡的晨光下闪耀。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 luster</td><td>/ˈlʌstər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>光泽，光彩</td><td>lose its luster<br>失去光泽</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Careful polishing restored the luster of the old silver bowl.  仔细打磨恢复了这只旧银碗的光泽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 scintillate</del></td><td>/ˈsɪntɪleɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>闪耀</td><td>scintillate with wit<br>闪耀着才智</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her essays scintillate with wit even when the subject seems dull.  即使题目乏味，她的文章也闪耀着才智。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sparkle</td><td>/ˈspɑːrkəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>闪耀，闪亮；生气</td><td>eyes sparkle with joy<br>双眼闪着喜悦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her eyes sparkled when she finally opened the present.  她终于打开礼物时眼睛闪闪发亮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 twinkle</td><td>/ˈtwɪŋkəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>闪烁，闪耀</td><td>stars twinkle overhead<br>星星在头顶闪烁</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thousands of stars twinkled above the quiet desert camp.  成千颗星星在寂静的沙漠营地上方闪烁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 winkle</td><td>/ˈwɪŋkəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词、及物动词</td><td>费力弄出，挖出；闪烁</td><td>winkle out the truth<br>把真相挖出来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reporters slowly winkled out the truth about the secret land deal.  记者们一点点挖出了那笔秘密土地交易的真相。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>gauche</b></td><td>/ɡoʊʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>笨拙的，缺乏社交经验的</td><td>a gauche remark<br>笨拙失礼的话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His gauche remark about her salary embarrassed the whole table.  他关于她薪水的笨拙失礼的话让整桌人都很尴尬。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 awkward</td><td>/ˈɔːkwərd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>笨拙的，不灵巧的；尴尬的</td><td>an awkward silence<br>尴尬的沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An awkward silence followed his remark about her divorce.  他谈到她离婚之后，出现了一阵尴尬的沉默。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 clumsy</td><td>/ˈklʌmzi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>笨拙的，不灵巧的</td><td>a clumsy attempt<br>笨拙的尝试</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 My clumsy attempt at juggling ended with three broken plates.  我笨拙地学抛球，结果打碎了三个盘子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crude</td><td>/ˈkruːd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗糙的，未加工的；粗俗的</td><td>crude oil<br>原油</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The furniture was crude but surprisingly sturdy and very cheap.  这家具做得粗糙，却意外结实又便宜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inept</td><td>/ɪˈnept/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚笨的，荒谬的；不称职的，无能力的</td><td>politically inept<br>政治上无能</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager was so inept at handling complaints that customers left in droves.  这位经理处理投诉如此无能，导致顾客大量流失。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 maladroit</td><td>/ˌmæləˈdrɔɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>笨拙的</td><td>a maladroit attempt<br>笨拙的尝试</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His maladroit attempt at an apology only made the situation worse.  他笨拙的道歉尝试只是让情况变得更糟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rustic</td><td>/ˈrʌstɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>乡下人，头脑简单的人；粗俗的</td><td>rustic manners<br>粗俗的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His rustic manners amused the guests at the elegant city dinner.  他粗俗的举止让城里雅致晚宴上的客人觉得好笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tactless</td><td>/ˈtæktləs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不机智的，笨拙的</td><td>a tactless comment<br>不得体的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One tactless comment about her age spoiled the whole evening.  一句关于她年龄的不得体评论毁了整个晚上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 graceful</td><td>/ˈɡreɪsfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>优雅的，优美的</td><td>a graceful dancer<br>姿态优雅的舞者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She made a graceful turn and landed without a sound.  她优雅地一个转身，落地时毫无声响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 3

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>gaudy</b></td><td>/ˈɡɔːdi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>俗丽的</td><td>gaudy jewelry<br>俗丽的首饰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She wore gaudy jewelry that clashed with her simple dress.  她戴着与朴素连衣裙不搭的俗丽首饰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 blatant</td><td>/ˈbleɪtnt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明显的，公然的</td><td>a blatant lie<br>赤裸裸的谎言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report exposed a blatant disregard for safety rules.  报告揭露了对安全规则的公然无视。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brazen</td><td>/ˈbreɪzn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>蛮横大胆的，厚颜无耻的；大胆自信地去面对或从事</td><td>a brazen lie<br>无耻的谎言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She told a brazen lie without even lowering her eyes.  她说了个无耻的谎言，连眼睛都没垂下。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flashy</td><td>/ˈflæʃi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>浮华的，华而不实的</td><td>a flashy sports car<br>浮华的跑车</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He arrived in a flashy suit that everyone immediately noticed.  他穿着一身浮华的西装到场，所有人立刻都注意到了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 garish</td><td>/ˈɡerɪʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>过于鲜艳的，过于张扬的</td><td>garish colors<br>过于鲜艳的颜色</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lobby was painted in garish colors that hurt the eyes.  大堂被漆成刺眼的过于鲜艳的颜色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 glaring</td><td>/ˈɡlerɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明显的，显眼的；耀眼的</td><td>a glaring error<br>明显的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is a glaring error on the very first printed page.  印刷的第一页上就有一个明显错误。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 meretricious</td><td>/ˌmerəˈtrɪʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>艳俗的，俗气的</td><td>meretricious ornament<br>艳俗的装饰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hotel lobby was crowded with meretricious ornaments of gilded plaster and plastic.  酒店大堂里堆满了镀金石膏和塑料做的艳俗装饰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tawdry</td><td>/ˈtɔːdri/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>俗丽的，花哨而庸俗的</td><td>tawdry decorations<br>花哨庸俗的装饰</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Tawdry decorations covered the walls of the cheap seaside hotel.  花哨庸俗的装饰铺满廉价海滨旅馆的墙壁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flamboyant</td><td>/flæmˈbɔɪənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>艳丽夺目的，炫耀的，充满装饰的</td><td>a flamboyant style<br>浮夸炫目的风格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The singer wore a flamboyant coat covered in mirrors and feathers.  歌手穿着一件缀满镜片和羽毛的艳丽夺目的外套。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 austere</td><td>/ɔːˈstɪr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>朴素的，朴实无华的</td><td>an austere room<br>简朴的房间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He slept in an austere room with a bed and one chair.  他睡在一间只有一张床和一把椅子的朴素房间里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 homely</td><td>/ˈhoʊmli/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>其貌不扬的，朴素简单的</td><td>a homely face<br>其貌不扬的脸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She had a homely but kind face that put strangers at ease.  她有一张其貌不扬却和善的脸，让陌生人感到自在。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 plain</td><td>/ˈpleɪn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、副词</td><td>完全的，十足的；朴素的，清楚的</td><td>plain stupid<br>简直愚蠢</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Ignoring the warning was just plain stupid.  忽视警告纯粹是愚蠢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>gauge</b></td><td>/ɡeɪdʒ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>测量标准；判定</td><td>a gauge of success<br>衡量成功的标准</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Ticket sales are a rough gauge of the show's success.  票房大致是衡量这部剧成功的标准。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 standard</td><td>/ˈstændərd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>标准的；标准，规范</td><td>standard procedure<br>标准流程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Checking IDs is standard procedure at the entrance.  在入口查验证件是标准流程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 benchmark</td><td>/ˈbentʃˌmɑːrk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>基准，衡量标准</td><td>set the benchmark<br>设定基准</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This test has become the benchmark for measuring urban air quality.  这项测试已成为衡量城市空气质量的基准。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 criterion</td><td>/kraɪˈtɪriən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>标准，准则</td><td>meet the main criterion<br>符合主要标准</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Cost was the main criterion for choosing this particular supplier.  成本是选择这家供应商的主要标准。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 measure</td><td>/ˈmeʒər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>衡量标准；测量；措施</td><td>a measure of success<br>衡量成功的尺度</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Test scores are a poor measure of a child's real ability.  考试分数不是衡量孩子真实能力的好尺度。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 touchstone</td><td>/ˈtʌtʃstoʊn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>试金石，衡量标准</td><td>the touchstone of quality<br>质量的试金石</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Public trust is the real touchstone of a good government.  公众信任是好政府真正的试金石。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 yardstick</td><td>/ˈjɑːrdˌstɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>衡量标准，准绳</td><td>use it as a yardstick<br>以此作为准绳</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Profit alone is a crude yardstick for judging any company.  仅看利润是评判公司的粗糙标准。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 assess</td><td>/əˈses/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>评估（重要性、尺寸、价值等）；征收费用（如罚款）</td><td>assess the damage<br>评估损失</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Engineers came to assess the damage caused by the summer flood.  工程师前来评估夏季洪水造成的损失。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 determine</td><td>/dəˈtɜːrmən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>判定，确定；决心</td><td>determine the cause<br>判定原因</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Scientists could not determine the cause of the sudden outbreak.  科学家无法判定这次突发疫情的原因。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 evaluate</td><td>/ɪˈvæljueɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>评价，估价，评估</td><td>evaluate the risks carefully<br>仔细评估风险</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Teachers evaluate each essay using the same detailed rubric.  教师用同一份细则评价每篇作文。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 figure</td><td>/ˈfɪɡjər/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>身材，身影；数字；人物</td><td>a slender figure<br>苗条的身材</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A tall figure appeared suddenly at the end of the corridor.  一个高大的身影突然出现在走廊尽头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scale</td><td>/ˈskeɪl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>标度，等级；规模</td><td>on a large scale<br>大规模地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rate your pain on a scale from one to ten.  请在一到十的标度上给你的疼痛评分。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>gear</b></td><td>/ɡɪr/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>调整（以配合）</td><td>gear production to demand<br>使生产配合需求</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The factory geared production to the demand of local buyers.  工厂调整生产以配合本地买家的需求。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 adapt</td><td>/əˈdæpt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>使适应；改编，改写</td><td>adapt to a new environment<br>适应新环境</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Immigrants must adapt to unfamiliar customs and a new language.  移民必须适应陌生的习俗和一门新语言。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 adjust</td><td>/əˈdʒʌst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>调整；使适应</td><td>adjust to a new job<br>适应新工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took her months to adjust to life in a new country.  她花了几个月才适应在新国家的生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fit</td><td>/ˈfɪt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>相符，适合；合适的</td><td>fit the description<br>符合描述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The suspect they arrested did not fit the witness's description.  他们逮捕的嫌疑人并不符合证人的描述。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 suit</td><td>/ˈsuːt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>适合，与……相配</td><td>suit your needs<br>适合你的需要</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We can arrange a time that suits your schedule better.  我们可以安排一个更适合你日程的时间。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tailor</td><td>/ˈteɪlər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使适合，专门制作</td><td>tailor a course to students<br>为学生定制课程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The training is tailored to each employee's actual duties.  培训是根据每位员工的实际职责量身定制的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>genial</b></td><td>/ˈdʒiːniəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>和蔼亲切的；温和宜人的</td><td>a genial host<br>亲切的主人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our genial neighbor invited the whole street over for dinner.  我们和蔼可亲的邻居请整条街的人来吃晚饭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 affable</td><td>/ˈæfəbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>和蔼的，温和的</td><td>an affable host<br>和蔼的主人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our affable neighbor greets everyone on the street by name.  我们和蔼的邻居在街上会叫出每个人的名字打招呼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 agreeable</td><td>/əˈɡriːəbl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人愉悦的；相一致的</td><td>an agreeable companion<br>一个讨人喜欢的同伴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She found him an agreeable companion on the long train journey.  她觉得他是长途火车旅程中一位讨人喜欢的同伴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gracious</td><td>/ˈɡreɪʃəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>和善的，亲切的</td><td>a gracious host<br>亲切的主人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was a gracious host who made every guest feel welcome.  她是位亲切的主人，让每位客人都感到受欢迎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mellow</td><td>/ˈmeloʊ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>温和的，圆熟的；变温和</td><td>grow mellow with age<br>随年岁渐趋温和</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He has grown much mellower since he became a grandfather.  当上祖父后他变得温和多了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nice</td><td>/ˈnaɪs/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>规矩得体的；令人愉快的</td><td>nice manners<br>得体的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is a nice boy who never gives his parents trouble.  他是个规矩的男孩，从不给父母添麻烦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pleasant</td><td>/ˈplezənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人愉快的；和善的</td><td>a pleasant surprise<br>令人愉快的惊喜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We spent a pleasant afternoon walking along the quiet river.  我们沿着静静的河边散步，度过了愉快的下午。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sweet</td><td>/ˈswiːt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>甜的；温和的，亲切的</td><td>in a sweet tone<br>以温和的语气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She spoke in a sweet voice that calmed the frightened child.  她用温柔的声音说话，安抚了害怕的孩子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 caustic</td><td>/ˈkɔːstɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>挖苦讽刺的，刻薄的</td><td>a caustic remark<br>刻薄的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His caustic remarks about the film upset the young director.  他对影片刻薄的评论让年轻导演很难过。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mordant</td><td>/ˈmɔːrdnt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>尖酸刻薄的</td><td>mordant wit<br>尖刻的机智</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His mordant wit amused readers but cost him several close friendships.  他尖刻的机智取悦了读者，却让他失去几段亲密友谊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>genteel</b></td><td>/dʒenˈtiːl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有教养的，不粗俗的</td><td>genteel manners<br>有教养的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her genteel accent betrayed an expensive and very traditional upbringing.  她文雅的口音透露出昂贵而传统的成长背景。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 courteous</td><td>/ˈkɜːrtiəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>礼貌的，谦恭有礼的</td><td>a courteous reply<br>礼貌的答复</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Even when customers shouted, the young clerk remained calm and courteous.  即使顾客大声嚷嚷，那位年轻店员仍然平静而有礼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 decent</td><td>/ˈdiːsənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>得体的，正派的</td><td>decent and respectful behavior<br>得体而恭敬的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is a decent man who always keeps his promises.  他是个正派的人，总是守承诺。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 decorous</td><td>/ˈdekərəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>得体端正的</td><td>decorous behavior at dinner<br>晚餐时得体的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Guests kept a decorous silence while the old bishop said grace.  老主教做餐前祷告时，客人们保持着得体的安静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mannerly</td><td>/ˈmænərli/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有礼貌的，举止得体的</td><td>a mannerly child<br>有礼貌的孩子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mannerly boy thanked everyone before he left the table.  这个有礼貌的男孩离席前向每个人道谢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 polite</td><td>/pəˈlaɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有礼貌的，客气的</td><td>make polite conversation<br>客套地寒暄</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It is polite to greet your neighbors in the morning.  早上向邻居问好是有礼貌的做法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 polished</td><td>/ˈpɑːlɪʃt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有教养的</td><td>polished manners<br>优雅得体的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His polished manners impressed everyone at the formal dinner.  他优雅得体的举止让正式晚宴上的每个人都印象深刻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 respectable</td><td>/rɪˈspektəbəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>体面的，值得尊敬的</td><td>a respectable middle-class family<br>体面的中产家庭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They came from a respectable family with an excellent local reputation.  他们出身于当地声誉极佳的体面家庭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 urbane</td><td>/ərˈbeɪn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>彬彬有礼的，文雅的</td><td>an urbane host<br>文雅的主人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our urbane host guided the conversation with easy wit and grace.  我们文雅的主人以轻松的机智和风度引导着谈话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 couth</del></td><td>/kuːθ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有教养的，文雅的</td><td>a couth young man<br>一个有教养的年轻人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was surprisingly couth for someone raised on the streets.  对一个街头长大的人来说，他出奇地有教养。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cultured</td><td>/ˈkʌltʃərd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有教养的，有修养的</td><td>a cultured gentleman<br>有教养的绅士</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was a cultured man who read poetry in four languages.  他是个有教养的人，能读四种语言的诗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 refined</td><td>/rəˈfaɪnd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>精炼的，纯净的；有教养的</td><td>refined sugar<br>精制糖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Refined sugar has been stripped of every impurity.  精制糖已去除了所有杂质。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 churlish</td><td>/ˈtʃɜːrlɪʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗鲁无礼的</td><td>a churlish refusal<br>无礼的拒绝</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It would be churlish to refuse such a generous gift.  拒绝如此丰厚的礼物就太无礼了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>germane</b></td><td>/dʒɜːrˈmeɪn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有关的，适当的</td><td>germane to the discussion<br>与讨论相关</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her question was germane to the discussion and changed our plan.  她的问题与讨论密切相关，改变了我们的计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 applicable</td><td>/ˈæplɪkəbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可用的，可行的；相关的，有关的</td><td>applicable to all students<br>适用于所有学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This rule is applicable to all students regardless of their year.  这条规定适用于所有学生，不论他们是哪个年级。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 apropos</td><td>/ˌæprəˈpoʊ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、介词</td><td>相关的；关于，有关</td><td>apropos of nothing<br>毫无来由地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Apropos of nothing, he asked whether I had ever been to Rome.  他毫无来由地问我是否去过罗马。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 apposite</td><td>/ˈæpəzɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>相关的，合适的</td><td>an apposite comment<br>恰当的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She made one apposite comment that settled the whole argument.  她一句恰当的评论就平息了整场争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pertinent</td><td>/ˈpɜːrtnənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>相关的，恰当的</td><td>pertinent to the discussion<br>与讨论相关的</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only evidence pertinent to the discussion should be presented today.  今天只应提出与讨论相关的证据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 relevant</td><td>/ˈreləvənt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有关系的，重要的</td><td>relevant to the case<br>与本案有关</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge ruled that the old letters were relevant to the case.  法官裁定那些旧信件与本案有关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 extraneous</td><td>/ɪkˈstreɪniəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关的，不重要的</td><td>extraneous information<br>无关的信息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Please delete all extraneous details from the summary.  请把摘要中所有无关的细节删掉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 irrelevant</td><td>/ˌɪˈreləvənt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无关的，不相干的</td><td>an irrelevant detail<br>无关的细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer objected because the question was completely irrelevant here.  律师提出反对，因为这个问题在此完全无关。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>gibe</b></td><td>/dʒaɪb/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>嘲弄</td><td>gibe at his accent<br>嘲弄他的口音</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The other students gibed at his accent until he stopped speaking.  其他学生嘲弄他的口音，直到他不再开口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deride</td><td>/dɪˈraɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>嘲弄，嘲笑</td><td>deride the proposal<br>嘲笑该提议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics derided the proposal as naive and completely out of touch.  批评者嘲笑该提案幼稚且完全脱离现实。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jeer</td><td>/ˈdʒɪr/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>嘲笑，讥讽</td><td>jeer at the loser<br>嘲笑失败者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crowd jeered at the player who missed the penalty.  人群嘲笑那名罚失点球的球员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ridicule</td><td>/ˈrɪdɪkjuːl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>嘲笑；讥讽；愚弄</td><td>an object of ridicule<br>被嘲笑的对象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Classmates began to ridicule him for his old shoes and worn coat.  同学们开始因他的旧鞋和破外套而嘲笑他。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mock</td><td>/ˈmɑːk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>假的，模拟的；嘲笑</td><td>a mock trial<br>模拟审判</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Students held a mock election to learn how voting works.  学生举行模拟选举以了解投票如何运作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scoff</td><td>/skɔːf/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>嘲笑</td><td>scoff at the idea<br>嘲笑这个想法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Colleagues used to scoff at the idea of working from home.  同事们过去常嘲笑在家办公的想法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sneer</td><td>/snɪr/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>（轻蔑地）嘲笑</td><td>sneer at his efforts<br>嘲笑他的努力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics sneered at his efforts, but he kept working on the design.  批评者嘲笑他的努力，他却继续完善设计。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 taunt</td><td>/tɔːnt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>嘲弄，讥讽；挑衅</td><td>taunt the loser<br>嘲弄失败者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The winning boys taunted the loser until a teacher intervened.  获胜的男孩们不停嘲弄输者，直到老师出面干预。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 respect</td><td>/rɪˈspekt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>尊敬，敬重；方面</td><td>show respect for<br>对……表示尊敬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Students should learn to respect opinions that differ from their own.  学生应学会尊重与自己不同的意见。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 revere</td><td>/rɪˈvɪr/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（尤指对神的）尊崇，尊敬</td><td>revered as a hero<br>被尊崇为英雄</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Students in the village still revere the teacher who founded their school.  村里的学生仍然尊崇创办他们学校的那位老师。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 venerate</td><td>/ˈvenəreɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>尊敬</td><td>venerate their ancestors<br>崇敬他们的祖先</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Villagers still venerate their ancestors with offerings twice each year.  村民每年两次以供品崇敬他们的祖先。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>giddy</b></td><td>/ˈɡɪdi/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻率不严肃的</td><td>a giddy schoolgirl<br>轻率的女学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The giddy schoolgirls laughed through the entire serious lecture.  那些轻率的女学生在整场严肃的讲座中笑个不停。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flighty</td><td>/ˈflaɪti/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻浮的，反复无常的</td><td>a flighty young man<br>轻浮的年轻人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She seemed flighty at first but proved remarkably reliable.  她起初显得轻浮，但事实证明非常可靠。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frivolous</td><td>/ˈfrɪvələs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻浮的；无关紧要的</td><td>a frivolous remark<br>轻率无谓的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge dismissed the frivolous lawsuit within a single morning.  法官在一个上午之内就驳回了这起无理的诉讼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 frothy</td><td>/ˈfrɔːθi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（内容等）欢乐轻佻、不严肃的</td><td>a frothy comedy<br>轻松不严肃的喜剧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The theater is showing a frothy comedy about summer romance.  剧院正在上演一部关于夏日恋情的轻松不严肃的喜剧。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 grave</td><td>/ˈɡreɪv/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>严肃的，严重的；坟墓</td><td>a grave expression<br>严肃的表情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The doctor's grave tone told us the news was bad.  医生严肃的语气告诉我们消息不好。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 serious</td><td>/ˈsɪriəs/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>严肃的，认真的；严重的</td><td>a serious student<br>认真的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is serious about becoming a research scientist.  她认真地想成为一名研究科学家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>gild</b></td><td>/ɡɪld/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>粉饰，美化；镀金</td><td>gild the truth<br>粉饰真相</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The memoir gilds the truth about his early business dealings.  这本回忆录粉饰了他早年商业交易的真相。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 polish</td><td>/ˈpɑːlɪʃ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>擦亮，磨光；润色</td><td>polish the wooden floor<br>把木地板擦亮</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She polished her essay carefully before sending it in.  她仔细润色文章后才交上去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 refine</td><td>/rɪˈfaɪn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>提纯，精炼；改进</td><td>refine crude oil<br>精炼原油</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The plant refines crude oil into gasoline and other useful products.  这家工厂把原油提炼成汽油和其他有用的产品。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 smooth</td><td>/ˈsmuːð/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>温和的，圆滑的；平滑的</td><td>a smooth talker<br>说话圆滑的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is a smooth talker who never offends anyone in negotiations.  他谈判时言辞圆滑，从不得罪任何人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>gist</b></td><td>/dʒɪst/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>要点</td><td>get the gist of<br>抓住…的要点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She summarized the gist of the long report in only three sentences.  她只用三句话就概括了那份长报告的要点。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 core</td><td>/ˈkɔːr/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>核心，要点</td><td>the core of the argument<br>论点的核心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 At the core of the dispute lies a simple money question.  争端的核心是一个简单的金钱问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 essence</td><td>/ˈesəns/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>本质，要点；精华</td><td>the essence of the argument<br>论证的要点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 In essence, the two proposals ask for exactly the same thing.  本质上，这两项提议要求的是同一件事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 kernel</td><td>/ˈkɜːrnəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>核心，要点；果核</td><td>the kernel of truth<br>一点真实的核心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There is a kernel of truth in this otherwise absurd rumor.  这个荒唐的谣言里有一点真实的核心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pivot</td><td>/ˈpɪvət/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>核心，关键；支点</td><td>the pivot of the plan<br>计划的关键</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Trade was the pivot on which the whole treaty turned.  贸易是整个条约的关键所在。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 quintessence</td><td>/kwɪnˈtesəns/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>精髓，典范</td><td>the quintessence of style<br>风格的精髓</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her writing is the quintessence of restraint and precision.  她的文字是克制与精确的精髓。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 substance</td><td>/ˈsʌbstəns/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>要旨，实质；物质</td><td>the substance of his speech<br>他讲话的要旨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The substance of her complaint was that nobody had asked her.  她抱怨的实质是没人问过她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
