<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cram</td><td>/ˈkræm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>塞满，填塞；死记硬背</td><td>cram the drawer with socks<br>把抽屉塞满袜子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She crammed all her winter clothes into one small suitcase.  她把所有冬衣塞进一个小箱子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 devour</td><td>/dɪˈvaʊər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>吞食，耗尽</td><td>devour the entire meal<br>吞掉整顿饭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hungry children devoured the entire meal in five minutes.  饥饿的孩子们五分钟内吞掉了整顿饭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gobble</td><td>/ˈɡɑːbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>狼吞虎咽</td><td>gobble down the meal<br>狼吞虎咽吃完饭</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children gobbled down their sandwiches and ran outside to play again.  孩子们狼吞虎咽吃完三明治，又跑到外面去玩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 guzzle</td><td>/ˈɡʌzl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>狂饮</td><td>guzzle a can of beer<br>狂饮一罐啤酒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He guzzled two bottles of soda before the game even started.  比赛还没开始他就狂饮了两瓶汽水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 quaff</td><td>/kwæf/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>痛饮；大口喝</td><td>quaff a mug of ale<br>大口喝下一杯麦酒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The thirsty travelers quaffed mugs of ale at the roadside inn.  口渴的旅人在路边客栈痛饮大杯麦酒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sate</td><td>/seɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使饱足，充分满足</td><td>sate one's hunger<br>充分满足饥饿感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A single bowl of thin soup was not enough to sate his hunger.  一碗清汤不足以满足他的饥饿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 swill</td><td>/ˈswɪl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>痛饮，大口喝；冲洗</td><td>swill down a beer<br>痛饮一杯啤酒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They swilled cheap beer all night in the noisy bar.  他们在嘈杂的酒吧里痛饮廉价啤酒一整夜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 nibble</td><td>/ˈnɪbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>小口咬</td><td>nibble at the cheese<br>小口啃干酪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mouse nibbled at the cheese we had left beside the kitchen door.  老鼠小口啃着我们放在厨房门边的干酪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>gossamer</b></td><td>/ˈɡɑːsəmər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>虚无缥缈的东西；轻薄的；薄弱的</td><td>gossamer wings<br>薄如蝉翼的翅膀</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her gossamer scarf floated on the breeze like a wisp of smoke.  她轻薄如蝉翼的围巾在微风中飘动，像一缕青烟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ether</td><td>/ˈiːθər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>苍穹，虚空；乙醚</td><td>vanish into the ether<br>消失于虚空</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His radio message simply vanished into the ether that night.  那晚他的无线电讯息就那样消失在虚空中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delicacy</td><td>/ˈdelɪkəsi/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>（外貌、结构等）精致</td><td>the delicacy of the lace<br>蕾丝的精致</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Everyone admired the delicacy of the carving on the ivory box.  大家都赞叹象牙盒上雕刻的精致。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 diaphanous</td><td>/daɪˈæfənəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>几乎透明的；模糊的，非实在的</td><td>a diaphanous silk veil<br>轻薄透明的丝纱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She wore a diaphanous scarf that barely shaded her from the sun.  她戴着一条几乎透明的纱巾，几乎挡不住阳光。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ethereal</td><td>/iˈθɪriəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻巧精致的；非物质的，精神（上）的</td><td>an ethereal beauty<br>轻灵飘逸的美</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her voice had an ethereal quality that hushed the entire hall.  她的嗓音有种轻灵飘逸的质感，让全场安静下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 filmy</td><td>/ˈfɪlmi/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>薄如蝉翼的，轻薄的</td><td>a filmy white curtain<br>轻薄的白窗帘</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A filmy curtain stirred gently in the warm evening breeze.  一幅轻薄的窗帘在温暖的晚风中轻轻飘动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 light</td><td>/ˈlaɪt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻微的，不严重的</td><td>a light meal<br>一顿便餐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He suffered only light injuries in the accident.  他在事故中只受了轻伤。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 insubstantial</td><td>/ˌɪnsəbˈstænʃəl/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非实在的；虚弱的</td><td>insubstantial evidence<br>站不住脚的证据</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His case rested on insubstantial rumor rather than real documents.  他的论据靠的是虚无的传言而非真实文件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tenuous</td><td>/ˈtenjuəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>站不住脚的，脆弱的；纤细的</td><td>a tenuous argument<br>站不住脚的论点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The lawyer's case rested on a tenuous argument about intent.  律师的诉讼建立在关于动机的站不住脚的论点上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 substance</td><td>/ˈsʌbstəns/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>要旨，实质；物质</td><td>the substance of his speech<br>他讲话的要旨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The substance of her complaint was that nobody had asked her.  她抱怨的实质是没人问过她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 entity</td><td>/ˈentəti/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>实体，独立存在物</td><td>a separate legal entity<br>独立的法律实体</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The subsidiary is now a separate legal entity from its parent.  这家子公司现在是独立于母公司的法律实体。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 heavy</td><td>/ˈhevi/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>重的，沉重的</td><td>too heavy to lift<br>重得抬不动</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The box was so heavy that two men could barely move it.  这箱子太重，两个人几乎搬不动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 leaden</td><td>/ˈledən/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>沉重的，无生气的；铅灰的</td><td>a leaden sky<br>铅灰色的天空</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He walked home with leaden steps after the painful defeat.  痛苦的失败后，他步履沉重地走回家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ponderous</td><td>/ˈpɑːndərəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>沉重的</td><td>ponderous machinery<br>笨重的机器</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Ponderous machinery filled the workshop and shook the floor all day.  笨重的机器占满车间，整天震动地板。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>grimy</b></td><td>/ˈɡraɪmi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>肮脏的，满是污垢的</td><td>grimy hands<br>肮脏的双手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mechanic wiped his grimy hands on an old checkered rag.  机械师用一块旧格子布擦了擦他肮脏的双手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 besmirched</td><td>/bɪˈsmɜrtʃt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>被弄脏的；名声受损的</td><td>a besmirched reputation<br>受损的名声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 One careless lie left his besmirched name beyond repair.  一句大意的谎言让他受损的名声无法挽回。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dingy</td><td>/ˈdɪndʒi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>昏暗的；肮脏的</td><td>a dingy little room<br>昏暗肮脏的小屋</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They rented a dingy little room whose single window faced a brick wall.  他们租了一间昏暗肮脏的小屋，唯一的窗户对着一堵砖墙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dusty</td><td>/ˈdʌsti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>满是灰尘的</td><td>dusty old books<br>满是灰尘的旧书</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We opened dusty boxes in the attic all afternoon.  我们整个下午在阁楼打开满是灰尘的箱子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 filthy</td><td>/ˈfɪlθi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>污秽的，极脏的</td><td>filthy old clothes<br>污秽的旧衣服</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The kitchen floor was filthy after the long noisy party.  漫长喧闹的聚会后厨房地板脏得一塌糊涂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dirty</td><td>/ˈdɜːrti/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>肮脏的；卑鄙的</td><td>dirty windows<br>脏污的窗子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children came home with dirty hands and happy faces.  孩子们回家时手很脏，脸上却很开心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 mucky</td><td>/ˈmʌki/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>污秽的，泥污的</td><td>a mucky farmyard<br>泥污的农家院</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their boots were mucky after crossing the flooded field.  穿过被淹的田地后他们的靴子满是污泥。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 muddy</td><td>/ˈmʌdi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>浑浊的，不清晰的；使难以理解</td><td>muddy the argument<br>把论点搅浑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Vague statistics only muddy the argument instead of settling it.  含糊的统计数据只会把论点搅浑，而不能解决争论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sordid</td><td>/ˈsɔːrdɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>肮脏的，不干净的；卑鄙的</td><td>a sordid little room<br>肮脏的小房间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They lived for years in a sordid little room without heat.  他们在没有暖气的肮脏小屋里住了多年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stained</td><td>/ˈsteɪnd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有污渍的；着色的</td><td>a stained tablecloth<br>有污渍的桌布</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He hid the stained shirt at the back of the drawer.  他把有污渍的衬衫藏在抽屉最里面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unclean</td><td>/ʌnˈkliːn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不洁的，脏的</td><td>unclean drinking water<br>不洁的饮用水</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Many villagers still rely on unclean water from the river.  许多村民仍靠河里不洁的水生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 clean</td><td>/ˈkliːn/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词、动词</td><td>干净的；打扫，清洗</td><td>a clean white shirt<br>干净的白衬衫</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Keep the wound clean and change the bandage every day.  保持伤口干净，每天更换绷带。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 immaculate</td><td>/ɪˈmækjələt/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>洁净无瑕的；完美无缺的</td><td>an immaculate record<br>毫无瑕疵的记录</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After thirty years of service his record remained absolutely immaculate.  服务三十年后，他的记录依然毫无瑕疵。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 spotless</td><td>/ˈspɑːtləs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>一点污迹也没有的</td><td>a spotless kitchen<br>一尘不染的厨房</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her spotless kitchen shone under the bright evening light.  她一尘不染的厨房在明亮的夜灯下发亮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 stainless</td><td>/ˈsteɪnləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无污渍的；不锈的</td><td>stainless steel pots<br>不锈钢锅具</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The stainless surface showed not one mark after cooking.  烹饪后这块不锈表面没有一点痕迹。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 unsoiled</del></td><td>/ˌʌnˈsɔɪld/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>未被弄脏的，洁净的</td><td>unsoiled bandages<br>洁净未污的绷带</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only one unsoiled napkin remained by the end of dinner.  晚餐结束时只剩一张未弄脏的餐巾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unstained</td><td>/ʌnˈsteɪnd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无污渍的；清白的</td><td>an unstained record<br>毫无污点的记录</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He retired after forty unstained years of public service.  他在四十年毫无污点的公职之后退休。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>gourmet</b></td><td>/ɡʊrˈmeɪ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>美食家</td><td>a gourmet restaurant<br>高级美食餐厅</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a serious gourmet, he traveled across three provinces for one soup.  作为一位认真的美食家，他为一道汤跨越了三个省。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bon vivant</td><td>/ˌbɑn viˈvɑnt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>讲究吃喝、会享受生活的人</td><td>a famous bon vivant<br>有名的享乐家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 As a bon vivant, he knew every good restaurant in Paris.  作为一个懂享受的人，他熟知巴黎每家好餐厅。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 connoisseur</td><td>/ˌkɑːnəˈsɜːr/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>鉴赏家（尤指艺术领域）；专家</td><td>a connoisseur of fine wine<br>美酒鉴赏家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is a connoisseur of fine wine and rare books.  他是美酒和珍本书的鉴赏家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 epicure</td><td>/ˈepɪkjʊr/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>美食家</td><td>a noted epicure<br>知名的美食家</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A noted epicure, he could name the region of any cheese he tasted.  他是知名的美食家，任何奶酪一尝就能说出产区。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 layman</td><td>/ˈleɪmən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>外行，非专业人士</td><td>in layman's terms<br>用通俗的话说</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Explain the diagnosis so a layman can understand it.  请解释诊断，让外行也能听懂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 6

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>grandeur</b></td><td>/ˈɡrændʒər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>宏伟，壮丽；（语言等）浮夸</td><td>the grandeur of the palace<br>宫殿的宏伟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Visitors fall silent before the sheer grandeur of the cathedral's vaulted interior.  在大教堂拱顶内部的宏伟面前，游客都不由沉默。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 magnificence</td><td>/mæɡˈnɪfɪsəns/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>壮丽，宏伟</td><td>the magnificence of the cathedral<br>大教堂的壮丽</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Visitors are struck by the magnificence of this ancient temple.  游客为这座古庙的壮丽所震撼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 augustness</del></td><td>/ɔːˈɡʌstnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>威严，庄严</td><td>the augustness of the ceremony<br>典礼的庄严</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The augustness of the old hall silenced every new visitor.  这座老厅的庄严让每位初来者都安静下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 brilliance</td><td>/ˈbrɪljəns/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>才华出众；光辉灿烂</td><td>the brilliance of his argument<br>他论证的精彩之处</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics praised the brilliance of the young director's first film.  批评家盛赞这位年轻导演处女作的精彩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 glory</td><td>/ˈɡlɔːri/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>感到自豪，得意；荣耀</td><td>glory in her success<br>为她的成功而得意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gloried in the praise his research finally received.  他为研究终于获得的赞誉而洋洋自得。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 majesty</td><td>/ˈmædʒəsti/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>雄伟，威严；陛下</td><td>the majesty of the mountains<br>群山的雄伟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Nothing prepares a traveler for the majesty of these falls.  这些瀑布的雄伟令旅人毫无准备。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nobility</td><td>/noʊˈbɪləti/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>高贵，崇高；贵族</td><td>nobility of character<br>品格的高贵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His quiet nobility of character impressed everyone who met him.  他沉静高贵的品格令每个见过他的人印象深刻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 resplendence</del></td><td>/rɪˈsplendəns/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>辉煌，绚丽</td><td>the resplendence of autumn<br>秋色的绚烂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The resplendence of the sunset silenced the whole tired group.  落日的辉煌让整群疲惫的人安静下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 splendor</td><td>/ˈsplendər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>壮丽，华美</td><td>in all its splendor<br>尽显其壮丽</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The restored palace now stands in all its former splendor.  修复后的宫殿如今重现昔日的壮丽。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 frivolousness</del></td><td>/ˈfrɪvələsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>轻浮，无聊琐碎</td><td>sheer frivolousness<br>纯粹的轻浮</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge criticized the frivolousness of the entire lawsuit.  法官批评了整场诉讼的无聊琐碎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>grandiloquent</b></td><td>/ɡrænˈdɪləkwənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>夸张的，说大话的</td><td>grandiloquent promises<br>夸张的许诺</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Voters grew tired of his grandiloquent promises about reform.  选民厌倦了他关于改革的夸张许诺。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rhetorical</td><td>/rɪˈtɔːrɪkl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>言辞华丽（却往往无内涵）的</td><td>empty rhetorical flourishes<br>空洞的华丽辞藻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His speech was full of rhetorical flourishes but offered no concrete plan.  他的演讲满是华丽辞藻，却没有提出任何具体计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bombastic</td><td>/bɑːmˈbæstɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>夸大的，浮夸的</td><td>bombastic rhetoric<br>浮夸的辞令</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His bombastic speech impressed nobody in the audience.  他浮夸的演讲没有打动任何听众。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inflated</td><td>/ɪnˈfleɪtɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>浮夸的，夸大的；膨胀的</td><td>inflated language<br>浮夸的措辞</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report was full of inflated claims about the new product.  报告里充满对新产品夸大的说法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 magniloquent</del></td><td>/mæɡˈnɪləkwənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>夸张的，辞藻浮夸的</td><td>a magniloquent style<br>浮夸的文风</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The preface is written in a magniloquent, old-fashioned style.  这篇序言以浮夸而老派的风格写成。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pretentious</td><td>/prɪˈtenʃəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>浮夸的，自命不凡的</td><td>pretentious prose<br>浮夸的文风</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His pretentious speech annoyed almost everyone in the audience.  他浮夸的演讲让几乎所有听众反感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 secretive</td><td>/ˈsiːkrətɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>隐秘的，遮遮掩掩的</td><td>secretive about his plans<br>对计划讳莫如深</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was oddly secretive about where all the money came from.  他对这些钱的来源异常遮掩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>grandiose</b></td><td>/ˈɡrændioʊs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>自命不凡的，浮夸的</td><td>grandiose plans<br>浮夸的计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His grandiose plans for the company never survived contact with reality.  他为公司制定的浮夸计划一遇现实便破灭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 extravagant</td><td>/ɪkˈstrævəɡənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>挥霍的；过度的，不必要的</td><td>extravagant spending<br>挥霍的开支</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their extravagant spending on parties left the charity nearly broke.  他们在宴会上的挥霍开支让这家慈善机构几乎破产。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flamboyant</td><td>/flæmˈbɔɪənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>艳丽夺目的，炫耀的，充满装饰的</td><td>a flamboyant style<br>浮夸炫目的风格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The singer wore a flamboyant coat covered in mirrors and feathers.  歌手穿着一件缀满镜片和羽毛的艳丽夺目的外套。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pompous</td><td>/ˈpɑːmpəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>浮夸的；傲慢的</td><td>a pompous speech<br>浮夸的演讲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The pompous speech bored everyone in the crowded town hall.  那场浮夸的演讲让拥挤的市政厅里每个人都厌烦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pretentious</td><td>/prɪˈtenʃəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>浮夸的，自命不凡的</td><td>pretentious prose<br>浮夸的文风</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His pretentious speech annoyed almost everyone in the audience.  他浮夸的演讲让几乎所有听众反感。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 humble</td><td>/ˈhʌmbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>卑微的，地位低下的；谦逊的</td><td>humble origins<br>卑微的出身</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He rose from humble origins to lead the company.  他出身卑微，最终领导了这家公司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>grandstand</b></td><td>/ˈɡrændstænd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>为了给人留下印象而表演，哗众取宠</td><td>grandstand for the cameras<br>对着镜头哗众取宠</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The senator was clearly grandstanding rather than negotiating in good faith.  这位参议员显然是在哗众取宠，而不是诚意谈判。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 act</td><td>/ˈækt/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>装样子，表演；行动</td><td>act for the crowd<br>为观众做样子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was only acting hurt to win the crowd's sympathy.  他只是装作受伤，以博取观众同情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 perform</td><td>/pərˈfɔːrm/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>表演；执行，履行</td><td>perform on stage<br>在舞台上表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young pianist will perform on stage for the first time.  这位年轻钢琴家将首次登台表演。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 pretend</td><td>/prɪˈtend/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>假装，装作</td><td>pretend to be asleep<br>装睡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children pretended not to hear their mother calling.  孩子们假装没听见母亲呼唤。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>grate</b></td><td>/ɡreɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>刮擦（以发出刺耳的声音）；骚扰，惹恼</td><td>grate on my nerves<br>让我心烦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her constant humming began to grate on everyone in the small office.  她不停的哼唱开始让小办公室里的每个人心烦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 abrade</td><td>/əˈbreɪd/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>磨损，擦伤；使烦躁</td><td>abrade the skin<br>擦伤皮肤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rough rope abraded the sailor's palms.  粗糙的绳子磨伤了水手的手掌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rasp</td><td>/ˈræsp/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>用粗锉磨；沙哑地说</td><td>rasp a rough edge<br>锉平粗糙的边缘</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He rasped the wood smooth before painting it.  他把木头锉平后再上漆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scratch</td><td>/ˈskrætʃ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>刮擦，抓挠；擦痕</td><td>scratch the surface<br>刮擦表面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cat scratched the wooden door all night long.  猫整夜抓挠着木门。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scrape</td><td>/ˈskreɪp/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>刮，擦；刮擦声</td><td>scrape the paint off<br>把油漆刮掉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He carefully scraped the old paint off the window frame.  他仔细地把窗框上的旧漆刮掉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 irritate</td><td>/ˈɪrɪteɪt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使恼怒；刺激</td><td>irritate the customers<br>使顾客恼火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Loud advertisements before every video irritate most of our customers.  每个视频前的大声广告让我们大多数客户感到恼火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 aggravate</td><td>/ˈæɡrəveɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>加重，恶化；激怒</td><td>aggravate the situation<br>使局势恶化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Running on the injury will only aggravate it.  带着伤跑步只会让伤势加重。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gall</td><td>/ɡɔːl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>（使）焦躁，激怒</td><td>galled by the remark<br>被那句话惹恼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His smug tone galled everyone sitting around the conference table.  他自鸣得意的语气激怒了围坐在会议桌旁的每个人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nettle</td><td>/ˈnetl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>惹怒</td><td>nettled by criticism<br>被批评惹怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was clearly nettled by the reporter's rude question.  记者无礼的提问显然惹恼了她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peeve</td><td>/piːv/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>打扰，惹恼</td><td>peeve the audience<br>惹恼观众</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Loud phone conversations on the train really peeve the other passengers.  火车上大声打电话确实惹恼其他乘客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 plague</td><td>/pleɪɡ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>瘟疫；折磨，使…痛苦</td><td>plagued by doubts<br>被疑虑折磨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Doubts about the decision plagued him for the rest of the year.  对那个决定的疑虑折磨了他余下的一整年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 provoke</td><td>/prəˈvoʊk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>激怒；驱使，激起</td><td>provoke an angry response<br>引发愤怒的回应</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His careless remark provoked an angry response from the whole audience.  他不慎的言论激起了全体观众的愤怒回应。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 vex</td><td>/veks/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使烦恼，使恼怒</td><td>vex the teacher<br>使老师恼怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Constant interruptions vexed the teacher throughout the long afternoon lesson.  不断的打扰让老师在漫长的下午课上十分恼怒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 soothe</td><td>/suːð/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>安慰，抚慰；减轻</td><td>soothe a crying baby<br>安抚哭闹的婴儿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Soft music helped soothe the restless patient through the long night.  轻柔的音乐帮助安抚了焦躁的病人度过漫长的夜晚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>gratify</b></td><td>/ˈɡrætɪfaɪ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使满足，使高兴</td><td>gratify a desire<br>满足某种欲望</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It gratified her to see her students succeed.  看到学生成功让她很欣慰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 appease</td><td>/əˈpiːz/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>安抚，平息；满足</td><td>appease the angry crowd<br>安抚愤怒的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company offered refunds to appease its angry customers.  公司提供退款以安抚愤怒的顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cater</td><td>/ˈkeɪtər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>迎合，满足；承办酒席</td><td>cater to popular taste<br>迎合大众口味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This magazine caters to readers who love old racing cars.  这本杂志迎合喜爱老赛车的读者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 content</td><td>/ˈkɑːntent/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>内容，内涵；使满足</td><td>the content of the letter<br>信的内容</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher praised the style but questioned the content of my essay.  老师赞赏我文章的文风，却质疑其内容。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 satisfy</td><td>/ˈsætɪsfaɪ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使满意；使确信</td><td>satisfy the skeptics<br>让怀疑者信服</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The extra evidence satisfied the skeptics who had doubted his story.  额外的证据让怀疑他说法的人信服了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 displease</td><td>/dɪsˈpliːz/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使不满，使不快</td><td>displease the audience<br>使观众不悦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The change in schedule displeased nearly all the parents.  日程的变动让几乎所有家长不满。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>gratuitous</b></td><td>/ɡrəˈtuːɪtəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无根据的，无理由的；无报酬的，免费的</td><td>gratuitous violence<br>毫无必要的暴力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film was criticized for its gratuitous violence and very thin plot.  这部电影因毫无必要的暴力和极单薄的情节而受到批评。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unfounded</td><td>/ənˈfaʊndɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>毫无根据的</td><td>unfounded rumors<br>毫无根据的谣言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company called the reports of bankruptcy completely unfounded.  公司称破产的报道完全没有根据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unjustified</td><td>/ʌnˈdʒʌstəfaɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无正当理由的</td><td>an unjustified attack<br>无理的攻击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The harsh criticism of her work was completely unjustified.  对她工作的严厉批评完全没有道理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unreasonable</td><td>/ənˈriːznəbəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不合理的，过分的</td><td>unreasonable demands<br>不合理的要求</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Asking staff to work three weekends running is unreasonable.  要求员工连续三个周末加班是不合理的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 baseless</td><td>/ˈbeɪsləs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无根据的，毫无凭据的</td><td>baseless accusations<br>无根据的指控</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company called the accusations baseless and refused to comment.  公司称这些指控毫无根据，并拒绝评论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 groundless</td><td>/ˈɡraʊndləs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>没有依据的</td><td>groundless accusations<br>毫无根据的指控</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court found the accusations entirely groundless and dismissed them.  法庭认定这些指控毫无根据，予以驳回。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 free</td><td>/ˈfriː/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、形容词</td><td>释放，解放；自由的</td><td>free the hostages<br>释放人质</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Negotiators finally persuaded the gunmen to free the hostages.  谈判者最终说服枪手释放了人质。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 complimentary</td><td>/ˌkɑːmpləˈmentəri/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>免费赠送的；赞美的</td><td>a complimentary breakfast<br>免费的早餐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hotel offers a complimentary breakfast to every overnight guest.  酒店为每位住宿客人提供免费早餐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unearned</td><td>/ʌnˈɜːrnd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非劳动所得的，不应得的</td><td>unearned income<br>非劳动所得收入</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most of his wealth was unearned inheritance from distant relatives.  他的大部分财富是远亲留下的非劳动所得遗产。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 voluntary</td><td>/ˈvɑːlənteri/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>自愿的，志愿的</td><td>voluntary participation<br>自愿参与</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Attendance at the evening seminar is entirely voluntary.  晚间研讨会完全自愿参加。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 Justified</td><td>/ˈdʒʌstəˌfaɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有正当理由的，合理的</td><td>fully justified anger<br>完全合理的愤怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her complaint was justified, and the store refunded the money.  她的投诉有理，商店退了钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 warranted</td><td>/ˈwɔːrəntɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有正当理由的，应得的</td><td>fully warranted criticism<br>完全有理的批评</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Given the delays, their anger seemed entirely warranted.  考虑到这些延误，他们的愤怒似乎完全合理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 merited</td><td>/ˈmerɪtɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>应得的，理所当然的</td><td>merited praise<br>应得的赞扬</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The award was merited praise for thirty years of quiet, patient teaching.  这个奖项是对三十年默默耐心教学的应得赞誉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>green</b></td><td>/ɡriːn/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无经验的</td><td>green recruits<br>毫无经验的新兵</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The green interns needed close supervision during their very first month.  这些毫无经验的实习生在最初一个月需要密切的指导。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inexperienced</td><td>/ˌɪnɪkˈspɪriənst/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>缺乏经验的，不熟练的</td><td>an inexperienced driver<br>缺乏经验的司机</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team was young and inexperienced but full of energy.  这支队伍年轻缺乏经验，却充满活力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 callow</td><td>/ˈkæloʊ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不老练的，不成熟的</td><td>a callow youth<br>不成熟的年轻人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The callow recruit asked questions that made the veterans laugh.  这名稚嫩的新兵问的问题让老兵们发笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fresh</td><td>/ˈfreʃ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>新颖的；新鲜的</td><td>a fresh approach<br>新颖的方法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team needs a fresh approach to this old problem.  团队需要用新颖的方法处理这个老问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 raw</td><td>/ˈrɔː/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无经验的；未加工的</td><td>a raw recruit<br>新手，生手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team was full of raw recruits with no real experience.  队里都是毫无实战经验的生手。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 unfledged</del></td><td>/ʌnˈflɛdʒd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>羽毛未齐的；未成熟的</td><td>an unfledged lawyer<br>尚不老练的律师</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The unfledged birds could not yet leave the nest.  这些羽毛未齐的雏鸟还不能离巢。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 young</td><td>/ˈjʌŋ/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>年轻的；缺乏经验的</td><td>young and inexperienced<br>年轻而缺乏经验</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was too young to handle such a difficult legal case.  她太年轻，处理不了这样棘手的法律案子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 experienced</td><td>/ɪkˈspɪriənst/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有经验的，老练的</td><td>an experienced guide<br>经验丰富的导游</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We hired an experienced nurse to care for my grandmother.  我们请了一位有经验的护士照顾我祖母。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 versed</td><td>/vɜːrst/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>熟知的；精通的</td><td>well versed in history<br>精通历史</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is well versed in both classical and modern poetry.  他对古典诗歌和现代诗歌都很精通。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>gregarious</b></td><td>/ɡrɪˈɡeriəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>爱社交的；群居的</td><td>a gregarious personality<br>爱社交的性格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Unlike his shy brother, he is gregarious and knows everyone in town.  与害羞的弟弟不同，他爱社交，认识镇上所有人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 convivial</td><td>/kənˈvɪviəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>欢快的，好交际的</td><td>a convivial atmosphere<br>欢快的气氛</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dinner had a convivial atmosphere that lasted until midnight.  晚宴的欢快气氛一直持续到午夜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 extroverted</td><td>/ˈekstrəˌvɜːrtɪd/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>外向的，性格开朗的</td><td>an extroverted student<br>外向的学生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her extroverted nature made her popular at every new school.  她外向的性格让她在每所新学校都很受欢迎。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 genial</td><td>/ˈdʒiːniəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>和蔼亲切的；温和宜人的</td><td>a genial host<br>亲切的主人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Our genial neighbor invited the whole street over for dinner.  我们和蔼可亲的邻居请整条街的人来吃晚饭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 outgoing</td><td>/ˈaʊtˌɡoʊɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>外向的，友好健谈的</td><td>an outgoing personality<br>外向的性格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His outgoing personality helped him make friends very quickly.  他外向的性格让他很快就交到朋友。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sociable</td><td>/ˈsoʊʃəbəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>好交际的，友善的</td><td>a sociable personality<br>好交际的性格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She is far more sociable than her quiet older brother.  她比她安静的哥哥爱交际得多。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 social</td><td>/ˈsoʊʃəl/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>爱交际的；社会的</td><td>a social animal<br>群居动物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Humans are social creatures who need regular company and talk.  人是需要经常陪伴与交谈的社会性生物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 aloof</td><td>/əˈluːf/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷淡的，冷漠孤傲的</td><td>remain aloof from<br>对…保持冷淡疏远</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He stayed aloof from office gossip and rarely joined lunch.  他对办公室的闲话保持冷淡，很少参加午餐。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 solitary</td><td>/ˈsɑːləˌteri/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>独居的，孤独的</td><td>a solitary life<br>独居的生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old writer led a solitary life high in the mountains.  这位老作家在高山上过着独居生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>grieve</b></td><td>/ɡriːv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词、及物动词</td><td>（使）感到悲伤</td><td>grieve for a lost friend<br>为逝去的朋友悲伤</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole village grieved for the fishermen lost in the autumn storm.  全村人都为在秋季风暴中失踪的渔民而悲伤。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 agonize</td><td>/ˈæɡənaɪz/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>（使）非常痛苦，苦苦思索</td><td>agonize over a decision<br>为决定苦苦纠结</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She agonized for weeks over whether to accept the job.  她为是否接受这份工作苦恼了好几周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 anguish</td><td>/ˈæŋɡwɪʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>极度痛苦，苦恼</td><td>in great anguish<br>极度痛苦之中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His face showed the anguish of a sleepless night.  他的脸上写着一夜无眠的痛苦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 suffer</td><td>/ˈsʌfər/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>受苦，遭受；容忍</td><td>suffer from a headache<br>受头痛折磨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thousands suffered from the drought that struck the region that summer.  那年夏天，数千人饱受该地区旱灾之苦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bemoan</td><td>/bɪˈmoʊn/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>哀叹，抱怨</td><td>bemoan his bad luck<br>哀叹自己运气不好</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Farmers bemoaned the loss of their crops after the flood.  洪水过后农民哀叹作物尽毁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 bewail</del></td><td>/bɪˈweɪl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>痛惜，哀叹</td><td>bewail her sad fate<br>哀叹她的悲惨命运</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The villagers gathered to bewail the loss of their homes.  村民聚在一起痛惜失去家园。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deplore</td><td>/dɪˈplɔːr/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>谴责，强烈反对</td><td>deplore the violence<br>谴责暴力行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Church leaders deplored the violence and called for immediate restraint.  教会领袖谴责暴力行为，呼吁立即克制。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 delight</td><td>/dɪˈlaɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使高兴，使愉快；愉悦</td><td>delight the audience<br>使观众高兴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young pianist delighted the audience with a lively encore.  这位年轻钢琴家用一首活泼的加演曲取悦了观众。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 exult in</td><td>/ɪɡˈzʌlt ɪn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>为…狂喜，因…欢欣</td><td>exult in the victory<br>为胜利而狂喜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The players exulted in their unexpected win over the champions.  球员们为意外战胜冠军而狂喜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 glory in</td><td>/ˈɡlɔːri ɪn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>为…自豪，因…欢欣</td><td>glory in his success<br>为他的成功而自豪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gloried in the praise that her young students received.  她为学生们获得的赞誉而欣喜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 joy</td><td>/ˈdʒɔɪ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>欢乐，喜悦</td><td>jump for joy<br>高兴得跳起来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The news of his recovery filled the whole family with joy.  他康复的消息让全家人充满喜悦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rejoice in</td><td>/rɪˈdʒɔɪs ɪn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>为…感到欣喜</td><td>rejoice in good fortune<br>为好运而欣喜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole village rejoiced in the news of her recovery.  全村人都为她康复的消息感到欣喜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 7

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>glamorous</b></td><td>/ˈɡlæmərəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的</td><td>a glamorous lifestyle<br>迷人的生活方式</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She left her quiet hometown for a glamorous life in Paris.  她离开宁静的家乡，去巴黎过迷人的生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 alluring</td><td>/əˈlʊrɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>诱人的，有魅力的</td><td>an alluring smile<br>迷人的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The alluring aroma of fresh bread drew us into the bakery.  新鲜面包诱人的香气把我们引进了面包店。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 appealing</td><td>/əˈpiːlɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>吸引人的</td><td>an appealing offer<br>有吸引力的提议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company made an appealing offer that she could hardly refuse.  公司提出了一个她几乎无法拒绝的诱人条件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 attractive</td><td>/əˈtræktɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有吸引力的，迷人的</td><td>an attractive offer<br>有吸引力的提议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company made him an attractive offer he could not refuse.  公司给了他一个无法拒绝的诱人条件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bewitching</td><td>/bɪˈwɪtʃɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的；令人着迷的</td><td>a bewitching smile<br>迷人的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave the photographer a bewitching smile over her shoulder.  她回头对摄影师露出迷人的微笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 captivating</td><td>/ˈkæptɪˌveɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的，令人着迷的</td><td>a captivating performance<br>令人着迷的表演</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her captivating voice held the entire audience in complete silence.  她迷人的歌声让全场观众鸦雀无声。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 charismatic</td><td>/ˌkærɪzˈmætɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有魅力的，有感召力的</td><td>a charismatic leader<br>有感召力的领袖</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The charismatic speaker convinced thousands to join his new movement.  这位有魅力的演讲者说服数千人加入他的新运动。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 charming</td><td>/ˈtʃɑːrmɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的，可爱的</td><td>a charming little village<br>迷人的小村庄</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We spent the afternoon in a charming village by the lake.  我们在湖边一个迷人的村庄度过了下午。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enchanting</td><td>/ɪnˈtʃæntɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的，醉人的</td><td>an enchanting melody<br>醉人的旋律</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The garden looked enchanting under the soft light of lanterns.  花园在灯笼柔和的光线下显得十分迷人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 engaging</td><td>/ɪnˈɡeɪdʒɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有吸引力的，讨人喜欢的</td><td>an engaging personality<br>讨人喜欢的性格</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His engaging manner made strangers feel comfortable within minutes.  他讨人喜欢的举止让陌生人几分钟内就放松下来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 entrancing</td><td>/ɪnˈtrænsɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人入迷的，使人陶醉的</td><td>an entrancing dance<br>令人入迷的舞蹈</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The dancer's entrancing movements kept every eye fixed on the stage.  舞者令人入迷的动作让所有目光都盯着舞台。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fascinating</td><td>/ˈfæsəˌneɪtɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的，极有趣的</td><td>a fascinating discovery<br>引人入胜的发现</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Insect behavior is a fascinating subject for young scientists.  昆虫行为对年轻科学家来说是极有趣的课题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 repellent</td><td>/rɪˈpelənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>令人厌恶的；驱虫剂</td><td>a repellent odor<br>令人厌恶的气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I found his arrogant remarks utterly repellent and left early.  我觉得他傲慢的言论令人极其厌恶，于是提前离开。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 repelling</td><td>/rəˈpelɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人反感的，令人厌恶的</td><td>a repelling appearance<br>令人反感的外表</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The swamp gave off a repelling smell of rotting plants.  沼泽散发出腐烂植物令人厌恶的气味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 repugnant</td><td>/rɪˈpʌɡnənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的</td><td>morally repugnant behavior<br>道德上可憎的行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Most readers found the cruelty of that character morally repugnant and unforgivable.  多数读者觉得那个角色的残忍在道德上令人厌恶，不可宽恕。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 repulsive</td><td>/rɪˈpʌlsɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的，恶心的</td><td>a repulsive sight<br>令人恶心的景象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rotting fish on the beach was absolutely repulsive.  沙滩上腐烂的鱼令人极其恶心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 revolting</td><td>/rɪˈvoʊltɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人厌恶的，恶心的</td><td>a revolting smell<br>令人恶心的气味</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A revolting smell came from the abandoned refrigerator in the yard.  院里那台废弃冰箱散出一股恶心的气味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>grin</b></td><td>/ɡrɪn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>咧嘴笑，咧嘴笑着表示</td><td>grin from ear to ear<br>笑得合不上嘴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He grinned broadly when the judges announced his name as the winner.  当评委宣布他获胜时，他开心地咧嘴大笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 beam</td><td>/ˈbiːm/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>笑容满面；光束</td><td>beam with pride<br>满脸自豪地微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The father beamed with pride as his daughter walked onstage.  女儿走上台时父亲笑得满脸自豪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 pout</td><td>/paʊt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>（尤指撅嘴或板着脸）表示不悦</td><td>pout in silence<br>板着脸不出声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She began to pout when her parents refused to buy the toy.  父母不肯买那个玩具时，她开始撅嘴表示不悦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>gripe</b></td><td>/ɡraɪp/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>抱怨</td><td>gripe about the food<br>抱怨饭菜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The passengers griped loudly about the delay for nearly a whole hour.  乘客们大声抱怨延误，抱怨了将近一个小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 carp</td><td>/kɑːrp/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>对小事吹毛求疵，挑剔，表现不满</td><td>carp at every detail<br>对每个细节都挑剔</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She carped at every detail of the seating arrangement.  她对座位安排的每个细节都挑剔。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fuss</td><td>/fʌs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>小题大做，抱怨；忙乱</td><td>make a fuss about<br>为…大惊小怪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Don't make a fuss about a tiny stain on the tablecloth.  别为桌布上一小块污渍小题大做。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grouch</td><td>/ˈɡraʊtʃ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>抱怨；牢骚满腹的人</td><td>grouch about the rules<br>抱怨规定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He grouched about the new rules but followed them anyway.  他抱怨新规定，但还是照做了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grouse</td><td>/ˈɡraʊs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>抱怨，发牢骚</td><td>grouse about long hours<br>抱怨工时太长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The crew groused about long hours during the harvest season.  收割季里工人们抱怨工时太长。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 grumble</td><td>/ˈɡrʌmbl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>（尤指低声地）抱怨，埋怨</td><td>grumble under one's breath<br>低声抱怨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The workers grumbled about the new rules but followed them anyway.  工人们低声抱怨新规定，但还是照办了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wail</td><td>/ˈweɪl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>痛哭，哀号；抱怨</td><td>wail in grief<br>悲痛地哀号</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mourners wailed as the coffin was lowered into the grave.  棺木下葬时，吊唁者们放声哀号。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 crow</td><td>/kroʊ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>得意地夸耀，自鸣得意</td><td>crow about the win<br>为获胜而得意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He crowed about his victory until even his closest friends grew tired.  他为胜利得意个不停，连最亲近的朋友都听厌了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 delight</td><td>/dɪˈlaɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>使高兴，使愉快；愉悦</td><td>delight the audience<br>使观众高兴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young pianist delighted the audience with a lively encore.  这位年轻钢琴家用一首活泼的加演曲取悦了观众。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rejoice</td><td>/rɪˈdʒɔɪs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>欣喜，喜悦</td><td>rejoice at the news<br>为消息而欣喜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole village rejoiced at the news of the safe return.  听到平安归来的消息，全村都欣喜不已。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>grisly</b></td><td>/ˈɡrɪzli/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人反感的，令人恐惧的</td><td>grisly details<br>令人恐惧的细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The newspaper spared its readers the grisly details of the accident.  报纸没有向读者披露事故中那些令人恐惧的细节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 appalling</td><td>/əˈpɔːlɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>骇人的，糟糕透顶的</td><td>appalling living conditions<br>骇人的居住条件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The workers lived in appalling conditions close to the mine.  工人们住在矿区附近骇人的条件中。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dreadful</td><td>/ˈdredfəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可怕的，糟透的</td><td>a dreadful mistake<br>可怕的错误</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She had a dreadful headache and went home early.  她头疼得厉害，很早就回家了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ghastly</td><td>/ˈɡæstli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可怕的；惨白的</td><td>a ghastly discovery<br>可怕的发现</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He turned a ghastly white when he heard the news.  他听到消息时脸色惨白。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gruesome</td><td>/ˈɡruːsəm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>可怕的，令人毛骨悚然的</td><td>gruesome details<br>可怕的细节</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The witness described the gruesome scene in a shaking voice.  证人用颤抖的声音描述了那可怕的场面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hideous</td><td>/ˈhɪdiəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非常丑陋的</td><td>a hideous mask<br>丑陋可怕的面具</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They tore down the hideous concrete tower that blocked the view.  他们拆掉了那座挡住视野的丑陋混凝土塔楼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 horrifying</td><td>/ˈhɔːrəˌfaɪɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人震惊恐惧的</td><td>horrifying footage<br>令人震惊的画面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rescuers described horrifying scenes inside the collapsed apartment block.  救援人员描述了倒塌公寓楼内令人震惊的景象。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 macabre</td><td>/məˈkɑːbrə/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>恐怖的</td><td>a macabre tale<br>恐怖的故事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He entertained the children with a macabre tale about a haunted mill.  他用一个关于鬼屋磨坊的恐怖故事逗孩子们开心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>groove</b></td><td>/ɡruːv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>享受，极其满意，过得快活</td><td>groove on the music<br>沉醉于音乐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole crowd was grooving to the band's long final song.  全场观众都沉醉在乐队那首长长的终曲里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 adore</td><td>/əˈdɔːr/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>热爱，极喜爱；崇拜</td><td>absolutely adore someone<br>非常喜爱某人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children adore their new puppy.  孩子们非常喜爱他们的新小狗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fancy</td><td>/ˈfænsi/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>喜爱，想要</td><td>fancy a cup of tea<br>想喝杯茶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do you fancy a walk along the river this evening?  今晚想不想去河边散散步？</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 savor</td><td>/ˈseɪvər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>品味，享受</td><td>savor every moment<br>品味每一刻</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She paused to savor the smell of fresh bread.  她停下来细细品味新鲜面包的香味。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 relish</td><td>/ˈrelɪʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>享受，喜爱；乐趣，兴味</td><td>with obvious relish<br>带着明显的享受</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He described the meal with obvious relish.  他津津有味地描述那顿饭。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 get off on</td><td>/ɡet ɔːf ɑn/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>从…中获得极大快感</td><td>get off on the applause<br>极享受这份掌声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He seems to get off on making other people nervous.  他好像很享受让别人紧张。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rejoice in</td><td>/rɪˈdʒɔɪs ɪn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>为…感到欣喜</td><td>rejoice in good fortune<br>为好运而欣喜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The whole village rejoiced in the news of her recovery.  全村人都为她康复的消息感到欣喜。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 revel in</td><td>/ˈrevl ɪn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>沉醉于，尽情享受</td><td>revel in the attention<br>尽情享受关注</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young actress reveled in the attention of the huge crowd.  年轻女演员尽情享受着人群的关注。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delight in</td><td>/dɪˈlaɪt ɪn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>喜爱，以…为乐</td><td>delight in teasing others<br>以逗弄别人为乐</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children delight in splashing through every puddle.  孩子们喜欢在每个水坑里踩水玩。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>grotesque</b></td><td>/ɡroʊˈtesk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>难看的</td><td>a grotesque figure<br>难看的形象</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mask was so grotesque that the youngest child began to cry.  那副面具太难看了，最小的孩子哭了起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hideous</td><td>/ˈhɪdiəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>非常丑陋的</td><td>a hideous mask<br>丑陋可怕的面具</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They tore down the hideous concrete tower that blocked the view.  他们拆掉了那座挡住视野的丑陋混凝土塔楼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 homely</td><td>/ˈhoʊmli/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>其貌不扬的，朴素简单的</td><td>a homely face<br>其貌不扬的脸</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She had a homely but kind face that put strangers at ease.  她有一张其貌不扬却和善的脸，让陌生人感到自在。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ill-favored</td><td>/ɪl ˈfeɪvərd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>相貌丑陋的</td><td>an ill-favored face<br>丑陋的面容</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ill-favored old man was surprisingly gentle with small children.  这位相貌丑陋的老人对小孩子出奇地温和。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 monstrous</td><td>/ˈmɑːnstrəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>巨大的；骇人的</td><td>a monstrous wave<br>巨浪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A monstrous wave rose above the tiny fishing boat.  一个巨浪在小渔船上方涌起。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unappealing</td><td>/ˌʌnəˈpiːlɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>没有吸引力的，不讨喜的</td><td>an unappealing dish<br>不讨喜的菜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The cold gray soup looked completely unappealing to all of us.  那碗冷灰色的汤在我们看来毫无食欲。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unattractive</td><td>/ˌʌnəˈtræktɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>没有吸引力的，难看的</td><td>an unattractive offer<br>没有吸引力的报价</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The job was well paid but otherwise thoroughly unattractive.  这份工作薪水不错，但其他方面毫无吸引力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 uncomely</del></td><td>/ʌnˈkʌmli/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不好看的，不雅观的</td><td>an uncomely dress<br>不好看的裙子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She wore an uncomely dress that hid her real charm.  她穿了件不好看的裙子，掩住了她真正的魅力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unsightly</td><td>/ʌnˈsaɪtli/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>难看的，刺眼的</td><td>unsightly stains<br>难看的污渍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Unsightly stains covered the wall behind the old kitchen sink.  难看的污渍覆盖了旧厨房水槽后的墙面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 aesthetic</td><td>/esˈθetɪk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>审美的，美的</td><td>aesthetic appeal<br>审美上的吸引力</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The building's aesthetic appeal outweighs its many practical problems.  这栋楼的美感胜过它的诸多实用缺陷。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 attractive</td><td>/əˈtræktɪv/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有吸引力的，迷人的</td><td>an attractive offer<br>有吸引力的提议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company made him an attractive offer he could not refuse.  公司给了他一个无法拒绝的诱人条件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 beautiful</td><td>/ˈbjuːtəfəl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>美丽的，美好的</td><td>a beautiful view<br>美丽的景色</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 From the hill we had a beautiful view of the whole bay.  从山上我们看到整个海湾的美丽景色。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 bonny</td><td>/ˈbɑːni/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>漂亮的，健康可爱的</td><td>a bonny baby<br>健康可爱的婴儿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their bonny little girl charmed everyone at the family party.  他们可爱的小女孩迷住了家庭聚会上的每个人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 comely</td><td>/ˈkʌmli/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>秀丽的，好看的</td><td>a comely young woman<br>秀丽的年轻女子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A comely young woman greeted us politely at the gate.  一位秀丽的年轻女子在门口礼貌地迎接我们。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fetching</td><td>/ˈfetʃɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的，好看的</td><td>a fetching hat<br>好看的帽子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She looked quite fetching in that simple blue summer dress.  她穿那件素净的蓝色夏裙相当好看。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 gorgeous</td><td>/ˈɡɔːrdʒəs/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极美的，绚丽的</td><td>a gorgeous sunset<br>绚丽的落日</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We watched a gorgeous sunset from the hotel balcony.  我们在酒店阳台上看了一场绚丽的落日。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 knockout</td><td>/ˈnɑːˌkaʊt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>击倒昏迷；击倒获胜</td><td>win by a knockout<br>以击倒对手获胜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The champion ended the fight with a knockout in round two.  冠军在第二回合以击倒结束了比赛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 ravishing</td><td>/ˈrævɪʃɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>极美的，令人陶醉的</td><td>a ravishing beauty<br>绝色的美人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She looked absolutely ravishing in her long silver evening gown.  她穿着银色晚礼长裙，美得令人陶醉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 seemly</td><td>/ˈsiːmli/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>得体的，合宜的</td><td>seemly conduct in public<br>公共场合得体的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The teacher expects seemly conduct from her students in public places.  老师期望学生在公共场所举止得体。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sightly</td><td>/ˈsaɪtli/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>好看的，悦目的</td><td>a sightly garden<br>悦目的花园</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They turned the empty lot into a sightly little garden.  他们把空地改成了一个悦目的小花园。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 stunning</td><td>/ˈstʌnɪŋ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>令人震惊的；极漂亮的</td><td>a stunning upset<br>令人震惊的逆转</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The underdog scored a stunning victory in the final round.  弱队在最后一轮取得令人震惊的胜利。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 taking</td><td>/ˈteɪkɪŋ/</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>迷人的，讨人喜欢的</td><td>a taking manner<br>讨人喜欢的举止</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The child had a taking manner that won everyone over.  这孩子讨人喜欢的举止赢得了所有人的心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 well-favored</td><td>/wel ˈfeɪvərd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>相貌好看的</td><td>a well-favored young man<br>相貌好看的年轻人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was a well-favored young man with easy country manners.  他是个相貌好看、举止随和的乡下青年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>grovel</b></td><td>/ˈɡrɑːvl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>卑躬屈膝</td><td>grovel before authority<br>在权威前卑躬屈膝</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He refused to grovel before the committee just to keep his post.  他拒绝为了保住职位而在委员会面前卑躬屈膝。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cringe</td><td>/ˈkrɪndʒ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>卑躬屈膝；畏缩</td><td>cringe before the boss<br>在老板面前卑躬屈膝</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The servant cringed whenever the duke raised his voice.  公爵一提高声音，仆人就畏缩起来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 creep</td><td>/kriːp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>缓慢地行进</td><td>creep along slowly<br>缓慢地爬行前进</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Traffic crept forward only a few meters at a time all morning.  整个上午车流每次只缓慢前进几米。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slither</td><td>/ˈslɪðər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>匍匐爬行，滑行</td><td>slither across the floor<br>在地上匍匐滑行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A snake slithered quietly across the warm stone path.  一条蛇静静地滑过温热的石径。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wriggle</td><td>/ˈrɪɡəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>扭动，蠕动前行</td><td>wriggle through the gap<br>扭身钻过缝隙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The boy wriggled through the narrow gap in the wooden fence.  男孩扭身钻过木栅栏的窄缝。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>grueling</b></td><td>/ˈɡruːəlɪŋ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>累人的，折磨人的</td><td>a grueling schedule<br>折磨人的日程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team endured a grueling schedule of twelve games in three weeks.  球队熬过了三周十二场比赛这种折磨人的赛程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 arduous</td><td>/ˈɑːrdʒuəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>难以做到的，费劲的</td><td>an arduous journey<br>艰苦的旅程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The climb proved far more arduous than any of them expected.  这次攀登远比他们任何人预想的都费劲。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 burdensome</td><td>/ˈbɜːrdənsəm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>繁重的，累人的</td><td>burdensome regulations<br>繁琐的规定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new paperwork proved burdensome for small rural clinics.  新的文书工作对乡村小诊所来说很繁重。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 laborious</td><td>/ləˈbɔːriəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>费力的，艰苦的；勤奋的</td><td>a laborious process<br>费力的过程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Copying the manuscript by hand proved a slow and laborious process.  手抄这份手稿是一个缓慢而费力的过程。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 onerous</td><td>/ˈɑːnərəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>繁重的，费力的</td><td>onerous responsibilities<br>繁重的责任</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Caring for the archive proved more onerous than the volunteers had expected.  照管这份档案比志愿者们预想的更费时费心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 taxing</td><td>/ˈtæksɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>繁重的，费力的</td><td>a taxing task<br>繁重的任务</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Caring for two infants proved a taxing task for the young couple.  照顾两个婴儿对这对年轻夫妻是繁重的任务。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 toilsome</del></td><td>/ˈtɔɪlsəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>辛苦的，费力的</td><td>toilsome labor<br>辛苦的劳作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Copying manuscripts by hand was toilsome work that ruined many eyes.  手抄书稿是毁坏许多人眼睛的辛苦活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 undemanding</td><td>/ˌʌndɪˈmændɪŋ/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>要求不高的，轻松的</td><td>an undemanding course<br>要求不高的课程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She chose an undemanding role so she could rest more.  她选了个轻松的角色以便多休息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 facile</td><td>/ˈfæsl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>容易做到的；肤浅轻率的</td><td>a facile explanation<br>肤浅的解释</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report offers a facile explanation for a very complex problem.  这份报告对一个非常复杂的问题给出了肤浅的解释。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>guile</b></td><td>/ɡaɪl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>狡猾，狡诈</td><td>full of guile<br>满是狡诈</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He won the long negotiation through patience rather than guile.  他靠耐心而不是狡诈赢下了这场漫长的谈判。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 artfulness</del></td><td>/ˈɑrtfəlnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>狡诈，工于心计</td><td>the artfulness of the plan<br>这计划的狡诈</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The artfulness of his questions trapped the witness completely.  他问题中的狡诈让证人彻底落入圈套。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 canniness</del></td><td>/ˈkæninəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>精明，机警</td><td>the canniness of a veteran trader<br>老练交易员的精明</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His canniness about market timing made him a fortune.  他对入市时机的精明判断让他发了财。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 craft</td><td>/ˈkræft/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>狡诈，诡计；手艺</td><td>use craft and cunning<br>使用狡诈手段</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He won the case by craft rather than by solid evidence.  他靠诡计而非确凿证据赢了官司。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 cunningness</del></td><td>/ˈkʌnɪŋnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>狡猾，狡诈</td><td>the cunningness of the fox<br>狐狸的狡猾</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her cunningness in business earned her both money and enemies.  她生意上的狡诈为她挣来金钱也挣来敌人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 deviousness</td><td>/ˈdiːviəsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>狡诈，不正当手段</td><td>political deviousness<br>政治上的狡诈</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His deviousness finally cost him the trust of every colleague.  他的狡诈最终让他失去每位同事的信任。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 slyness</del></td><td>/ˈslaɪnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>狡猾，诡诈</td><td>a hint of slyness<br>一丝狡猾</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 There was a hint of slyness in his friendly smile.  他友善的笑容里有一丝狡猾。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 subtleness</del></td><td>/ˈsʌtlnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>狡诈；微妙，精巧</td><td>the subtleness of his tactics<br>他战术的狡诈</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The subtleness of the trap fooled even the veteran detectives.  这个陷阱的精巧连老练的警探都骗过了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 wiliness</del></td><td>/ˈwaɪlinəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>狡猾，诡计多端</td><td>known for his wiliness<br>以狡猾出名</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old trader's wiliness made every buyer extremely careful.  这位老商人的狡猾让每个买家都格外小心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 artlessness</del></td><td>/ˈɑrtləsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>率真，毫无心机</td><td>childlike artlessness<br>孩童般的率真</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her artlessness in conversation made strangers trust her quickly.  她谈话中的率真让陌生人很快信任她。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 forthrightness</td><td>/ˈfɔːrθraɪtnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>直率，坦诚</td><td>admired for her forthrightness<br>因坦率受人钦佩</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her forthrightness made negotiations faster and far less painful.  她的坦率让谈判更快，也少了许多痛苦。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 ingenuousness</del></td><td>/ɪnˈdʒenjuəsnəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>天真，坦率无城府</td><td>youthful ingenuousness<br>年少的坦率</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His ingenuousness was refreshing among so many calculating people.  在众多精于算计的人中，他的坦率令人耳目一新。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sincerity</td><td>/sɪnˈserəti/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>真诚，诚挚</td><td>speak with sincerity<br>真诚地说话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 No one doubted the sincerity of her apology that evening.  那天晚上没人怀疑她道歉的真诚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 manipulative</td><td>/məˈnɪpjəˌleɪtɪv/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>善于操控他人的</td><td>a manipulative boss<br>善于操控人的上司</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her manipulative behavior slowly drove all her old friends away.  她操控人的行为慢慢让所有老朋友都离开了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>glean</b></td><td>/ɡliːn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>收集</td><td>glean information from reports<br>从报告中收集信息</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Researchers gleaned useful details from hundreds of old letters stored in the attic.  研究者从阁楼上数百封旧信中收集到有用的细节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 accumulate</td><td>/əˈkjuːmjəleɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>不及物动词、及物动词</td><td>逐渐增长，积累</td><td>accumulate wealth<br>积累财富</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Dust had accumulated on the shelves during our absence.  我们离开期间架子上积了灰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 amass</td><td>/əˈmæs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>积累，聚集（大量）</td><td>amass a fortune<br>积累财富</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Over thirty years she amassed an astonishing collection of maps.  三十多年间她积累了数量惊人的地图收藏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 assemble</td><td>/əˈsembəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>聚集，集合；组装</td><td>assemble a crowd<br>聚集起人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Villagers assembled in the square to hear the announcement.  村民聚集在广场上听那则公告。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 collect</td><td>/kəˈlekt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>收集，聚集，收取</td><td>collect rainwater<br>收集雨水</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Dust collects quickly on shelves in this dry climate.  在这种干燥气候里灰尘很快就积在架子上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 congregate</td><td>/ˈkɑːŋɡrəˌɡeɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>聚集，集合</td><td>congregate in the hall<br>聚集在大厅里</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Students congregate near the fountain between morning classes.  上午课间学生们聚集在喷泉附近。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 garner</td><td>/ˈɡɑːrnər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>获得，积累</td><td>garner support<br>获得支持</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The proposal garnered support from both major parties.  该提案获得了两大党的支持。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 8

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>gull</b></td><td>/ɡʌl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗</td><td>gull the unwary<br>欺骗粗心的人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The swindler gulled dozens of elderly investors out of their savings.  骗子欺骗了数十位老年投资者，骗走他们的积蓄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bamboozle</td><td>/bæmˈbuzəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗，蒙骗</td><td>bamboozle the customers<br>欺骗顾客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The salesman bamboozled them into buying a worthless extended warranty.  推销员骗他们买了毫无价值的延保。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 beguile</td><td>/bɪˈɡaɪl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗，诱骗；使着迷</td><td>beguile the audience<br>令观众着迷</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The magician could beguile an audience for a full hour.  这位魔术师能让观众着迷整整一小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cozen</td><td>/ˈkʌzn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>诱骗</td><td>cozen him into agreeing<br>诱骗他同意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The swindler cozened elderly widows out of their modest life savings.  那个骗子诱骗年老的寡妇们交出微薄的积蓄。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delude</td><td>/dɪˈluːd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗，使误信</td><td>delude oneself<br>自欺欺人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do not delude yourself into thinking the danger has passed.  别欺骗自己，以为危险已经过去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dupe</td><td>/duːp/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>欺骗</td><td>dupe trusting investors<br>欺骗轻信的投资者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The scheme duped trusting investors out of nearly a million dollars.  这个骗局欺骗轻信的投资者，骗走近百万美元。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fake out</td><td>/feɪk ˈaʊt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>欺骗，蒙骗，晃过</td><td>fake out the defender<br>晃过防守球员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The guard faked out two defenders and scored quite easily.  这名后卫晃过两名防守者，轻松得分。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gaff</td><td>/ˈɡæf/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>骗局，欺骗手段；鱼钩</td><td>spot the gaff<br>看出这个骗局</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He finally saw through the gaff of the smiling salesman.  他终于看穿了那位笑脸推销员的骗局。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hoax</td><td>/hoʊks/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>欺骗</td><td>an elaborate hoax<br>精心设计的骗局</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The photograph turned out to be an elaborate hoax created by students.  那张照片原来是学生们精心设计的骗局。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hoodwink</td><td>/ˈhʊdwɪŋk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗</td><td>hoodwink the voters<br>欺骗选民</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company hoodwinked customers with promises it could never possibly keep.  这家公司用根本无法兑现的承诺欺骗顾客。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 snooker</td><td>/ˈsnʊkər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗，诱骗</td><td>snooker into a deal<br>诱骗做成交易</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They snookered the old man into signing a worthless contract.  他们诱骗那位老人签了一份毫无价值的合同。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 string along</td><td>/strɪŋ əˈlɔːŋ/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>吊着哄骗，敷衍拖延</td><td>string him along for weeks<br>吊着他好几周</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company strung him along for months without any real offer.  公司吊着他好几个月，却没有真正的录用。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 take in</td><td>/teɪk ˈɪn/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>欺骗；收留；领会</td><td>be taken in by<br>被…欺骗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Don't be taken in by his charming manner and generous promises.  别被他迷人的举止和大方的承诺骗了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 undeceive</del></td><td>/ˌʌndɪˈsiːv/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使醒悟，使不再受骗</td><td>undeceive the public<br>使公众醒悟</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Someone must undeceive her before she invests everything in that scheme.  必须有人让她醒悟，别把全部钱投进那个计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>groundless</b></td><td>/ˈɡraʊndləs/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>没有依据的</td><td>groundless accusations<br>毫无根据的指控</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The court found the accusations entirely groundless and dismissed them.  法庭认定这些指控毫无根据，予以驳回。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 baseless</td><td>/ˈbeɪsləs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无根据的，毫无凭据的</td><td>baseless accusations<br>无根据的指控</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company called the accusations baseless and refused to comment.  公司称这些指控毫无根据，并拒绝评论。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 foundationless</del></td><td>/faʊnˈdeɪʃənləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>毫无根据的</td><td>foundationless rumors<br>毫无根据的谣言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report dismissed the claim as completely foundationless gossip.  报告认定这一说法是毫无根据的流言。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unfounded</td><td>/ənˈfaʊndɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>毫无根据的</td><td>unfounded rumors<br>毫无根据的谣言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company called the reports of bankruptcy completely unfounded.  公司称破产的报道完全没有根据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unreasonable</td><td>/ənˈriːznəbəl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不合理的，过分的</td><td>unreasonable demands<br>不合理的要求</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Asking staff to work three weekends running is unreasonable.  要求员工连续三个周末加班是不合理的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unsubstantiated</td><td>/ˌʌnsəbˈstænʃieɪtɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>未经证实的，无根据的</td><td>unsubstantiated claims<br>未经证实的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The judge rejected the unsubstantiated claims for lack of proof.  法官因缺乏证据驳回了这些未经证实的主张。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 unwarranted</td><td>/ʌnˈwɔːrəntɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>无正当理由的，不必要的</td><td>unwarranted interference<br>无理的干涉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her fear of the final exam turned out to be unwarranted.  她对期末考试的担忧结果证明毫无必要。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 Justified</td><td>/ˈdʒʌstəˌfaɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有正当理由的，合理的</td><td>fully justified anger<br>完全合理的愤怒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her complaint was justified, and the store refunded the money.  她的投诉有理，商店退了钱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 reasonable</td><td>/ˈriːzənəbəl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>合理的，通情达理的</td><td>a reasonable request<br>合理的请求</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That seems like a perfectly reasonable request.  那似乎是完全合理的要求。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 reasoned</td><td>/ˈriːzənd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>经过推理的，理由充分的</td><td>a reasoned argument<br>有理有据的论证</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She presented a reasoned case for keeping the library open.  她提出了有理有据的理由要求图书馆继续开放。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 substantiated</td><td>/səbˈstænʃˌieɪtɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>已证实的，有依据的</td><td>a substantiated report<br>已获证实的报告</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only substantiated complaints will be forwarded to the ethics committee.  只有已证实的投诉才会转交伦理委员会。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 valid</td><td>/ˈvælɪd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有效的；有根据的</td><td>a valid objection<br>有根据的反对意见</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Your ticket is valid for travel on any weekday this month.  你的票在本月任何工作日出行均有效。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 well-founded</td><td>/ˌwɛl ˈfaʊndɪd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有充分根据的</td><td>a well-founded fear<br>有充分根据的担忧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their fear of another flood proved to be well-founded.  他们对再次洪水的担忧被证明有充分根据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 well-grounded</td><td>/wel ˈɡraʊndɪd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>有充分根据的；基础扎实的</td><td>a well-grounded fear<br>有充分根据的担忧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their suspicion about the contract proved well-grounded after all.  他们对合同的怀疑最终证明是有根据的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>gush</b></td><td>/ɡʌʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>喷涌而出；滔滔不绝地说</td><td>gush from a broken pipe<br>从破裂的水管喷涌而出</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Water gushed from the broken pipe and flooded the basement.  水从破裂的水管喷涌而出，淹了地下室。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 drool</td><td>/ˈdruːl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>垂涎；过分夸赞</td><td>drool over the new car<br>对新车垂涎不已</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Fans drooled over every photo of the new sports car.  粉丝们对新跑车的每张照片都垂涎不已。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 effuse</del></td><td>/ɪˈfjuːz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>倾泻，热情倾吐</td><td>effuse warmth and praise<br>倾泻般地热情赞美</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She effused praise for every painting in the small gallery.  她对小画廊里的每幅画都赞不绝口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 enthuse</td><td>/ɪnˈθuːz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>热情地说；使热情</td><td>enthuse about the trip<br>热情谈论这次旅行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He enthused about the trip for the entire evening.  他整晚都在热情地谈这次旅行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fuss</td><td>/fʌs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>小题大做，抱怨；忙乱</td><td>make a fuss about<br>为…大惊小怪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Don't make a fuss about a tiny stain on the tablecloth.  别为桌布上一小块污渍小题大做。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rave</td><td>/reɪv/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>狂热赞扬；（发疯般地）怒吼</td><td>rave about the film<br>对影片赞不绝口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Critics raved about the film for weeks after its quiet premiere.  影片低调首映后，评论家连续数周对它赞不绝口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 slobber</td><td>/ˈslɑːbər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>流口水；过分表露感情</td><td>slobber over the baby<br>对婴儿过分亲热</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The big dog slobbered all over my clean white shirt.  那只大狗把口水流满了我干净的白衬衫。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>gust</b></td><td>/ɡʌst/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>（感情）爆发；一阵强风</td><td>a gust of anger<br>一阵怒气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A sudden gust of anger swept over him when he read the letter.  读到那封信时，一阵怒气突然涌上他心头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 burst</td><td>/ˈbɜːrst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>爆裂；突发，爆发</td><td>burst into flames<br>突然燃起大火</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old pipe burst and flooded the basement within minutes.  旧管子爆裂，几分钟就淹了地下室。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 ebullition</del></td><td>/ˌebəˈlɪʃn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>（感情）爆发；沸腾</td><td>an ebullition of joy<br>一阵喜悦的爆发</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His calm speech ended in a sudden ebullition of anger.  他平静的讲话以突然爆发的怒气收场。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 eruption</td><td>/ɪˈrʌpʃən/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>喷发；爆发</td><td>a volcanic eruption<br>火山喷发</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The eruption buried three villages under ash and molten rock.  这次喷发把三个村庄埋在火山灰和熔岩下。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 explosion</td><td>/ɪkˈsploʊʒən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>爆发；爆炸</td><td>an explosion of laughter<br>爆发的笑声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her joke caused an explosion of laughter in the crowded hall.  她的玩笑在挤满人的大厅里引起一阵爆笑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 flush</td><td>/flʌʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词、不及物动词</td><td>充裕的，有钱的；冲洗</td><td>flush with money<br>钱很充裕</td></tr>

</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gush</td><td>/ɡʌʃ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>喷涌而出；滔滔不绝地说</td><td>gush from a broken pipe<br>从破裂的水管喷涌而出</td></tr>

</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>guzzle</b></td><td>/ˈɡʌzl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>狂饮</td><td>guzzle a can of beer<br>狂饮一罐啤酒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He guzzled two bottles of soda before the game even started.  比赛还没开始他就狂饮了两瓶汽水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 gulp</td><td>/ˈɡʌlp/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>吞饮，大口地咽</td><td>gulp down the water<br>大口把水喝下</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He gulped down a glass of water and ran outside.  他大口喝下一杯水就跑了出去。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 quaff</td><td>/kwæf/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>痛饮；大口喝</td><td>quaff a mug of ale<br>大口喝下一杯麦酒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The thirsty travelers quaffed mugs of ale at the roadside inn.  口渴的旅人在路边客栈痛饮大杯麦酒。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 swig</td><td>/ˈswɪɡ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>大口喝；一大口</td><td>take a swig of juice<br>大口喝一口果汁</td></tr>
