</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 booze</td><td>/ˈbuːz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>酗酒；酒</td><td>booze all night<br>整夜酗酒</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They boozed all night and then slept through the morning.  他们整夜酗酒，然后一觉睡到中午。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 soak</td><td>/soʊk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使…湿透</td><td>soak the beans overnight<br>把豆子浸泡一夜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Soak the dried beans overnight before cooking them the next morning.  把干豆浸泡一夜，第二天早上再煮。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tipple</td><td>/ˈtɪpəl/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>常喝酒，小酌；酒</td><td>tipple in the evening<br>傍晚小酌</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old sailor tippled quietly by the fire every evening.  这位老水手每晚都在火炉边静静小酌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hack</b></td><td>/hæk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>雇佣文人；乱砍；开辟</td><td>hack through the jungle<br>在丛林中砍出通路</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novelist dismissed his critics as hacks writing for money alone.  这位小说家把批评他的人贬为只为钱写作的雇佣文人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hackneyed</b></td><td>/ˈhæknid/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈腐的，缺乏创新的</td><td>a hackneyed phrase<br>陈腐的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speech relied on hackneyed images of struggle and triumph.  这篇演讲依赖于陈腐的奋斗与胜利意象。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 banal</td><td>/bəˈnɑːl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非原创的，陈腐的</td><td>banal remarks<br>陈腐的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The film's ending was so banal that the audience laughed.  这部电影的结尾陈腐得让观众笑了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 cliché</del></td><td>/kliːˈʃeɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>陈词滥调，老套的说法</td><td>an overused cliché<br>用滥的陈词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Saying that time heals everything has become a tired cliché.  说时间治愈一切已成为老套的陈词滥调。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 commonplace</td><td>/ˈkɑːmənpleɪs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>平凡的；老生常谈</td><td>a commonplace observation<br>老生常谈的看法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Video calls have become commonplace in ordinary office life.  视频通话在日常办公中已很平常。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hack</td><td>/hæk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>雇佣文人；乱砍；开辟</td><td>hack through the jungle<br>在丛林中砍出通路</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novelist dismissed his critics as hacks writing for money alone.  这位小说家把批评他的人贬为只为钱写作的雇佣文人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 threadbare</td><td>/ˈθredber/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈腐的；破旧的</td><td>a threadbare excuse<br>老掉牙的借口</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He offered the same threadbare excuse for arriving late again.  他又用同一个老掉牙的借口解释迟到。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trite</td><td>/traɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈腐的，陈词滥调的</td><td>a trite observation<br>陈腐的见解</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speech relied on trite phrases that moved nobody in the hall.  那篇演讲满是陈腐的套话，没能感动会场里的任何人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 well-worn</td><td>/wel ˈwɔːrn/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>陈腐的；用旧的</td><td>a well-worn phrase<br>陈腐的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The speaker repeated a well-worn joke about airline food.  演讲者又讲了个关于飞机餐的老掉牙笑话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stereotyped</td><td>/ˈsteriətaɪpt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>老套的，刻板的</td><td>stereotyped characters<br>刻板的人物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The novel is full of stereotyped characters and predictable plots.  这部小说充满刻板人物和可预测的情节。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fresh</td><td>/ˈfreʃ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>新颖的；新鲜的</td><td>a fresh approach<br>新颖的方法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team needs a fresh approach to this old problem.  团队需要用新颖的方法处理这个老问题。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 offbeat</td><td>/ˌɔːfˈbiːt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不平常的</td><td>an offbeat sense of humor<br>别致的幽默感</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His offbeat sense of humor won over an audience that had arrived skeptical.  他别致的幽默感赢得了原本抱着怀疑到场的观众。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 original</td><td>/əˈrɪdʒənl/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>创新的；最初的，最早的</td><td>an original idea<br>有创意的想法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her proposal was genuinely original, unlike the tired ideas we usually hear.  她的提案确实新颖，不像我们常听到的那些陈旧想法。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 novel</td><td>/ˈnɑːvl/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>新奇的</td><td>a novel approach<br>新奇的方法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her novel approach to the old problem surprised even the senior researchers.  她对这个老问题的新奇方法连资深研究员都感到意外。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>halcyon</b></td><td>/ˈhælsiən/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>宁静的，平静的</td><td>halcyon days<br>宁静美好的日子</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He often talked about the halcyon days of his youth.  他常谈起年轻时那些宁静美好的日子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hushed</td><td>/ˈhʌʃt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>寂静的，静下来的</td><td>a hushed room<br>静寂的房间</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The hushed audience waited for the pianist to begin playing.  安静的观众等着钢琴家开始演奏。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 peaceful</td><td>/ˈpiːsfəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>和平的，平静的</td><td>a peaceful settlement<br>和平的解决方案</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Both sides finally chose a peaceful path out of conflict.  双方最终选择了走出冲突的和平道路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 placid</td><td>/ˈplæsɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>冷静的，淡定的</td><td>a placid temperament<br>沉静的性情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His placid temperament made him ideal for this very stressful job.  他沉静的性情使他非常适合这份高压工作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 serene</td><td>/səˈriːn/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>平静的，安详的</td><td>a serene smile<br>安详的微笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She watched the serene surface of the lake at dawn without speaking.  她默默看着黎明时宁静的湖面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tranquil</td><td>/ˈtræŋkwəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>平静的，安宁的</td><td>a tranquil lake<br>平静的湖面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We spent a tranquil afternoon reading beside the quiet river.  我们在静静的河边度过了一个安宁的下午。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 untroubled</td><td>/ʌnˈtrʌbəld/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>平静的，未受扰的</td><td>untroubled water<br>平静的水面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her face looked untroubled despite all the bad news.  尽管坏消息不断，她的神情依然平静。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 tempestuous</td><td>/temˈpestʃuəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>狂暴的，动荡不安的</td><td>a tempestuous relationship<br>剧烈动荡的关系</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their tempestuous marriage ended after only two stormy years.  他们剧烈动荡的婚姻仅两年就结束了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 stormy</td><td>/ˈstɔːrmi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>暴风雨的；激烈的</td><td>a stormy meeting<br>激烈的会议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The stormy meeting lasted until well after midnight.  这场激烈的会议一直开到午夜之后。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 agitated</td><td>/ˈædʒəˌteɪtɪd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>焦躁不安的，激动的</td><td>become visibly agitated<br>明显焦躁起来</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She grew agitated when the police refused to answer her questions.  警察拒绝回答她的问题时，她变得焦躁不安。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inclement</td><td>/ɪnˈklemənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>（天气等）恶劣的</td><td>inclement weather<br>恶劣的天气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ceremony moved indoors because of unusually inclement weather.  由于天气异常恶劣，典礼改在室内举行。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 restless</td><td>/ˈrestləs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>焦躁不安的；不平静的</td><td>a restless night<br>不安宁的一夜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spent a restless night listening to the wind rattle the windows.  他度过了一个不平静的夜晚，听着风摇窗户作响。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 rough</td><td>/ˈrʌf/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>粗糙的，粗暴的；艰苦的</td><td>a rough surface<br>粗糙的表面</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The road grew rough as we climbed into the hills.  我们爬上山时路变得坎坷不平。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 turbulent</td><td>/ˈtɜːrbjələnt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>动荡的</td><td>a turbulent period<br>动荡的时期</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They married during a turbulent period of war and economic collapse.  他们在战争与经济崩溃的动荡时期结了婚。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unquiet</td><td>/ʌnˈkwaɪət/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不平静的，不安的</td><td>an unquiet mind<br>不安的心绪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He spent an unquiet night waiting for the test results.  他不安地度过一夜，等着检测结果。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unsettled</td><td>/ʌnˈsetəld/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>动荡的，多变的；未定的</td><td>unsettled weather<br>多变的天气</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The unsettled weather ruined all our plans for the weekend.  多变的天气打乱了我们全部周末计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hale</b></td><td>/heɪl/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>强壮的，健壮的</td><td>hale and hearty<br>身体强健，精神饱满</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 At eighty he remains hale enough to walk five miles a day.  八十岁的他依然强健，每天能走五英里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bouncing</td><td>/ˈbaʊnsɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>活泼健康的，精力充沛的</td><td>a bouncing baby boy<br>健康活泼的男婴</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave birth to a bouncing baby boy last spring.  去年春天她生下一个健康活泼的男婴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fit</td><td>/ˈfɪt/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、形容词</td><td>相符，适合；合适的</td><td>fit the description<br>符合描述</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The suspect they arrested did not fit the witness's description.  他们逮捕的嫌疑人并不符合证人的描述。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 robust</td><td>/roʊˈbʌst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>精力充沛的；强壮的，健康的</td><td>robust good health<br>强健的好身体</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 At eighty he remains robust enough to work in the garden daily.  他八十岁了，身体依然强健，每天还能在园中劳作。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sound</td><td>/saʊnd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>牢固的；健康强壮的；（逻辑）严谨的</td><td>sound financial advice<br>稳妥的理财建议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her lawyer gave sound financial advice that saved the family thousands.  她的律师给出稳妥的理财建议，为家里省下数千元。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 well-conditioned</td><td>/wel kənˈdɪʃnd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>身体强健的，状态良好的</td><td>a well-conditioned athlete<br>体能良好的运动员</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only well-conditioned runners finished the race in that heat.  在那样的高温下只有体能良好的跑者跑完了比赛。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 wholesome</td><td>/ˈhoʊlsəm/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有益身心健康的</td><td>wholesome home-cooked food<br>有益健康的家常饭菜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children grew strong on wholesome food and plenty of fresh air.  孩子们靠有益健康的食物和充足的新鲜空气长得很结实。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 effete</td><td>/ɪˈfiːt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>衰弱的，衰落的</td><td>an effete aristocracy<br>衰落的贵族阶层</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An effete aristocracy clung to its titles long after the wealth had vanished.  衰落的贵族阶层在财富早已消失后仍死守头衔。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 infirm</td><td>/ɪnˈfɜːrm/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>虚弱的；不果断的，优柔寡断的</td><td>old and infirm<br>年老体弱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her grandmother grew too infirm to climb the stairs without help.  她的祖母身体太虚弱，没人帮忙就爬不了楼梯。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 anemic</td><td>/əˈniːmɪk/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>缺乏力量、活力、精神的</td><td>anemic economic growth<br>疲弱的经济增长</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sales growth was anemic despite the expensive advertising campaign.  尽管广告投入巨大，销售增长依然疲弱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wan</td><td>/wɑːn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>苍白的，病态的</td><td>a wan smile<br>苍白无力的一笑</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She gave us a wan smile and went back to bed.  她苍白无力地笑了一下，又回床上躺着了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 decrepit</td><td>/dɪˈkrepɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>虚弱的，衰老的，破旧的</td><td>a decrepit old building<br>一栋破旧的老楼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They lived for years in a decrepit house with no running water.  他们在一栋没有自来水的破旧房子里住了多年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 blighted</td><td>/ˈblaɪtɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>枯萎的，遭毁坏的</td><td>blighted crops<br>枯萎的庄稼</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Blighted crops left the whole valley without any income.  枯萎的庄稼让整个山谷失去收入。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>halfbaked</b></del></td><td>/ˌhæfˈbeɪkt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>考虑不周的，不成熟的</td><td>a halfbaked plan<br>考虑不周的计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They abandoned the halfbaked scheme after a single disastrous trial run.  经过一次灾难性的试运行，他们放弃了那个考虑不周的方案。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 daffy</td><td>/ˈdæfi/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>傻的，荒唐的</td><td>a daffy idea<br>荒唐的主意</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His daffy idea about selling ice in winter amused everyone.  他冬天卖冰的荒唐主意把大家逗乐了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 daft</td><td>/dæft/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚蠢的，傻的</td><td>a daft suggestion<br>愚蠢的建议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Selling the only truck in winter was a truly daft decision.  在冬天卖掉唯一的卡车实在是个愚蠢的决定。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dippy</td><td>/ˈdɪpi/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚蠢的，傻乎乎的</td><td>a dippy suggestion<br>愚蠢的建议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She made a dippy suggestion that nobody at all took seriously.  她提了个愚蠢的建议，根本没人当真。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 harebrained</td><td>/ˈherˌbreɪnd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>轻率愚蠢的</td><td>a harebrained scheme<br>轻率愚蠢的计划</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their harebrained scheme to get rich quickly failed badly.  他们快速致富的轻率计划惨败。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 half-witted</td><td>/hæf ˈwɪtɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>愚笨的，头脑简单的</td><td>a half-witted remark<br>愚笨的言论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He made a half-witted remark that embarrassed the whole team.  他说了句愚笨的话，让全队难堪。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 preposterous</td><td>/prɪˈpɑːstərəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>荒谬的，极其反常的</td><td>a preposterous claim<br>荒唐的说法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The idea that he acted alone is preposterous.  说他独自行动的说法荒谬至极。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 judicious</td><td>/dʒuˈdɪʃəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的，慎重的</td><td>a judicious choice<br>明智的选择</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A judicious use of savings carried the family through two difficult years.  明智地使用积蓄让这家人度过了两个艰难的年头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 prudent</td><td>/ˈpruːdnt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的；小心谨慎的，审慎的</td><td>a prudent investment<br>一笔审慎的投资</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It would be prudent to save part of every paycheck.  每份工资都存下一部分才是明智之举。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sagacious</td><td>/səˈɡeɪʃəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>睿智的，有远见的</td><td>a sagacious adviser<br>睿智的顾问</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His sagacious advice saved the family business from certain ruin.  他睿智的建议救家族生意于必败之境。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sapient</td><td>/ˈseɪpiənt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>聪明的，有洞察力的</td><td>a sapient observation<br>有洞察力的评论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A few sapient remarks from the editor saved the entire chapter.  编辑几句有洞察力的话救了整整一章。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sensible</td><td>/ˈsensəbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的，合理的</td><td>a sensible choice<br>明智的选择</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Wearing boots was a sensible choice in that mud.  在那种泥地里穿靴子是明智的选择。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sound</td><td>/saʊnd/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>牢固的；健康强壮的；（逻辑）严谨的</td><td>sound financial advice<br>稳妥的理财建议</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her lawyer gave sound financial advice that saved the family thousands.  她的律师给出稳妥的理财建议，为家里省下数千元。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 wise</td><td>/ˈwaɪz/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>明智的，有智慧的</td><td>a wise decision<br>明智的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It was wise of her to save money before quitting the job.  她辞职前先存钱，很明智。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 9

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hallmark</b></td><td>/ˈhɔːlmɑːrk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>典型的特征</td><td>the hallmark of quality<br>品质的标志</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Careful craftsmanship is the hallmark of every piece she makes.  精心的手工是她每一件作品的典型特征。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 ensign</td><td>/ˈensən/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>旗帜；标志，徽记</td><td>raise the ship's ensign<br>升起船旗</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Sailors raised the ensign as the ship left the harbor.  船离港时水手升起了船旗。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 impresa</del></td><td>/ɪmˈpreɪzə/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>纹章图案，徽记</td><td>a family impresa<br>家族徽记</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The knight's shield bore an impresa of a golden lion.  骑士的盾上有一头金狮的徽记。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 logo</td><td>/ˈloʊɡoʊ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>标志，徽标</td><td>the company logo<br>公司标志</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new logo appears on every product the company sells.  新标志出现在公司销售的每件产品上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 symbol</td><td>/ˈsɪmbəl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>象征，符号</td><td>a symbol of peace<br>和平的象征</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The white dove has long been a symbol of peace.  白鸽长久以来是和平的象征。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 totem</td><td>/ˈtoʊtəm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>图腾，象征物</td><td>a tribal totem<br>部落图腾</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The carved totem stood at the entrance of the old village.  雕刻的图腾柱立在老村口。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trademark</td><td>/ˈtreɪdˌmɑːrk/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>商标；标志性特征</td><td>a registered trademark<br>注册商标</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 That wide black hat became the singer's personal trademark.  那顶黑色宽边帽成了这位歌手的标志。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 uncharacteristic feature</td><td>/ˌʌnkerəktəˈrɪstɪk ˈfiːtʃər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>非典型的特征</td><td>a truly uncharacteristic feature<br>极不典型的特征</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Silence was an uncharacteristic feature of his usually lively lectures.  沉默是他平素生动讲课中很不典型的特征。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hallow</b></td><td>/ˈhæloʊ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>尊敬，把…视为神圣</td><td>hallow the ancient site<br>将古址奉为神圣</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Generations of pilgrims have hallowed this quiet mountain spring.  世代朝圣者把这泓山泉视为神圣。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 consecrate</td><td>/ˈkɑːnsəˌkreɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使神圣；奉献</td><td>consecrate the new church<br>为新教堂祝圣</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bishop consecrated the small stone church last Sunday morning.  主教上周日上午为这座小石教堂祝圣。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 sacralize</del></td><td>/ˈseɪkrəlaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使神圣化</td><td>sacralize the ancient site<br>使古迹神圣化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Local custom gradually sacralized the old spring on the hillside.  当地习俗渐渐把山坡上的老泉神圣化了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sanctify</td><td>/ˈsæŋktɪfaɪ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使神圣，使圣化</td><td>sanctify the temple ground<br>使庙地成为圣地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The bishop came to sanctify the ground where the chapel would stand.  主教前来使这片将建礼拜堂的土地成为圣地。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 desecrate</td><td>/ˈdesɪkreɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>亵渎，玷污</td><td>desecrate a holy shrine<br>亵渎神圣的殿堂</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Vandals desecrated the old cemetery and overturned a dozen stones.  破坏者亵渎了那座老墓园，推倒了十几块墓碑。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 deconsecrate</del></td><td>/diːˈkɑnsɪkreɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>撤销…的神圣地位</td><td>deconsecrate the old chapel<br>撤销旧教堂的神圣地位</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The diocese decided to deconsecrate the empty stone chapel.  教区决定撤销这座空石教堂的神圣地位。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 desacralize</del></td><td>/diːˈseɪkrəlaɪz/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使去神圣化</td><td>desacralize the ritual<br>使仪式去神圣化</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Modern tourism has slowly desacralized this once holy mountain.  现代旅游慢慢让这座曾经的圣山失去了神圣性。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 desanctify</del></td><td>/diːˈsæŋktɪfaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使不再神圣</td><td>desanctify the shrine<br>使神龛不再神圣</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new regime tried to desanctify every royal monument.  新政权试图让每座王室纪念物不再神圣。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hallucination</b></td><td>/həˌluːsɪˈneɪʃn/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>幻觉</td><td>suffer hallucinations<br>出现幻觉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The high fever gave him vivid hallucinations that lasted through the night.  高烧让他产生了持续整夜的鲜明幻觉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 chimera</td><td>/kaɪˈmɪrə/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>虚构的事物，幻觉</td><td>a mere chimera<br>纯粹的幻想</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Lasting peace seemed a mere chimera to the exhausted soldiers.  对疲惫的士兵来说，持久和平仿佛只是幻想。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 daydream</td><td>/ˈdeɪˌdriːm/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>白日梦，幻想</td><td>lost in a daydream<br>陷入白日梦中</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He sat by the window lost in a daydream about sailing away.  他坐在窗边，沉浸在扬帆远行的白日梦里。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 delusion</td><td>/dɪˈluːʒn/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>错觉，妄想</td><td>labor under a delusion<br>抱着错误的幻想</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He clung to the delusion that the company would soon rehire him.  他固执地抱着公司很快会重新雇用他的妄想。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fancy</td><td>/ˈfænsi/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>喜爱，想要</td><td>fancy a cup of tea<br>想喝杯茶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Do you fancy a walk along the river this evening?  今晚想不想去河边散散步？</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 figment</td><td>/ˈfɪɡmənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>虚构的事物，幻觉</td><td>a figment of imagination<br>凭空想象之物</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The threatening letter was a figment of his own imagination.  那封威胁信是他自己凭空想象出来的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 illusion</td><td>/ɪˈluːʒən/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>错觉，幻想</td><td>an optical illusion<br>视觉错觉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mirrors create the illusion that the room is twice as large.  镜子造成了房间大了一倍的错觉。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 truth</td><td>/ˈtruːθ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>真相，真实</td><td>tell the truth<br>说出真相</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 It took ten years for the truth about the accident to emerge.  这场事故的真相花了十年才浮出水面。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 verity</td><td>/ˈverəti/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>真理，真实</td><td>an eternal verity<br>永恒的真理</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The play explores eternal verities about love and human loss.  这部戏探讨关于爱与失去的永恒真理。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>ham-handed</b></td><td>/ˌhæm ˈhændɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>笨手笨脚的，不圆滑的</td><td>a ham-handed apology<br>笨拙的道歉</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His ham-handed attempt to help only made things worse.  他笨拙的帮忙只让事情更糟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 awkward</td><td>/ˈɔːkwərd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>笨拙的，不灵巧的；尴尬的</td><td>an awkward silence<br>尴尬的沉默</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 An awkward silence followed his remark about her divorce.  他谈到她离婚之后，出现了一阵尴尬的沉默。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 handless</td><td>/ˈhændləs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>笨手笨脚的，不灵巧的</td><td>handless with tools<br>使用工具很笨拙</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He is completely handless in the kitchen and burns almost everything.  他在厨房里笨手笨脚，几乎什么都烧糊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 heavy-handed</td><td>/ˌhevi ˈhændɪd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>笨拙的；严厉专断的</td><td>heavy-handed policing<br>粗暴的执法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The government's heavy-handed response angered many peaceful protesters.  政府的强硬做法激怒了许多和平抗议者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 maladroit</td><td>/ˌmæləˈdrɔɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>笨拙的</td><td>a maladroit attempt<br>笨拙的尝试</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His maladroit attempt at an apology only made the situation worse.  他笨拙的道歉尝试只是让情况变得更糟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 unhandy</del></td><td>/ʌnˈhændi/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不便使用的；笨手笨脚的</td><td>an unhandy tool<br>不好用的工具</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This unhandy old ladder is difficult to carry upstairs.  这把不好用的旧梯子很难搬上楼。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 deft</td><td>/deft/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>灵巧的，熟练的</td><td>a deft touch<br>娴熟的手法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 With a few deft strokes the artist finished the small sketch.  画家几笔灵巧的勾画便完成了那幅小素描。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 dexterous</td><td>/ˈdekstrəs/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>动作灵活的；头脑灵活的</td><td>dexterous fingers<br>灵巧的手指</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The surgeon's dexterous hands worked steadily for more than six hours.  这位外科医生灵活的双手稳稳地工作了六个多小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 handy</td><td>/ˈhændi/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>方便的；就在手边的</td><td>come in handy<br>派上用场</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Keep a flashlight handy in case the power fails tonight.  把手电筒放在手边，以防今晚停电。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sure-handed</td><td>/ʃʊr ˈhændɪd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>手法稳健熟练的</td><td>a sure-handed surgeon<br>手法稳健的外科医生</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The sure-handed pilot landed safely in heavy crosswinds.  这位手法稳健的飞行员在强侧风中安全着陆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 adroit</td><td>/əˈdrɔɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>熟练的，灵巧的</td><td>adroit at negotiation<br>擅长谈判</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She proved remarkably adroit at handling hostile questions from reporters.  她在应对记者的敌意提问时表现得非常娴熟。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hammer</b></td><td>/ˈhæmər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>屡次重申</td><td>hammer home the point<br>反复强调这一点</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach hammered home the importance of defense at every single practice.  教练在每一次训练中都反复强调防守的重要性。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hamper</b></td><td>/ˈhæmpər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>妨碍；阻碍；束缚</td><td>hamper the rescue effort<br>妨碍救援工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy snow hampered the rescue teams trying to reach the stranded climbers.  大雪妨碍了试图接近被困登山者的救援队。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cramp</td><td>/ˈkræmp/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>限制，束缚；痉挛</td><td>cramp his style<br>束缚他的发挥</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Lack of money cramped every plan the young director had.  资金短缺束缚了这位年轻导演的每个计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 encumber</td><td>/ɪnˈkʌmbər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>阻碍；妨碍</td><td>encumber the project<br>妨碍这个项目</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Too many rules encumber small businesses in this country.  太多规定阻碍了这个国家的小企业。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 fetter</td><td>/ˈfetər/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>束缚</td><td>the fetters of tradition<br>传统的束缚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Heavy family obligations fettered her ambition for many long years.  沉重的家庭责任多年来束缚了她的雄心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 handicap</td><td>/ˈhændikæp/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>妨碍，使不利；障碍</td><td>handicap the team<br>使球队处于不利</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Poor equipment handicapped the researchers from the very start.  设备不足从一开始就妨碍了研究人员。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hinder</td><td>/ˈhɪndər/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>阻碍，妨碍</td><td>hinder progress<br>妨碍进展</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Poor lighting hindered the search for survivors.  照明不足妨碍了对生还者的搜寻。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 impede</td><td>/ɪmˈpiːd/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>妨碍，阻碍</td><td>impede progress<br>阻碍进展</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Thick fog impeded the rescue team's progress.  浓雾阻碍了救援队的前进。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stymie</td><td>/ˈstaɪmi/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>阻碍，妨碍</td><td>stymie an investigation<br>阻碍调查</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Missing records stymied the investigation for months.  档案缺失使调查停滞了数月。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 trammel</td><td>/ˈtræməl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>束缚，限制</td><td>the trammels of custom<br>习俗的束缚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Old rules trammel the creativity of young designers here.  旧规矩束缚了这里年轻设计师的创造力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 facilitate</td><td>/fəˈsɪlɪteɪt/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使变容易，促进</td><td>facilitate the discussion<br>促进讨论</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new airport will facilitate tourism throughout the whole region.  新机场将促进整个地区的旅游业。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 aid</td><td>/ˈeɪd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>帮助，援助</td><td>come to someone's aid<br>前来帮助某人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Neighbors came to her aid within minutes of the fire.  火灾发生几分钟内邻居们就来帮忙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 assist</td><td>/əˈsɪst/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>动词</td><td>帮助，协助</td><td>assist with the work<br>协助工作</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Two nurses assisted the surgeon during the operation.  两名护士在手术中协助医生。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 help</td><td>/ˈhelp/</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>帮助，助益</td><td>a great help<br>很大的帮助</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Your advice was a great help during the difficult week.  在那困难的一周里你的建议帮了大忙。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hamstring</b></td><td>/ˈhæmstrɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使无效，使无力</td><td>hamstring the investigation<br>使调查无法进行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Budget cuts hamstrung the agency's ability to investigate serious public complaints.  预算削减使该机构调查严重公众投诉的能力大打折扣。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cripple</td><td>/ˈkrɪpəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使严重受损，削弱</td><td>cripple the economy<br>严重削弱经济</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The strike crippled the city's transport system for a week.  罢工使该市交通系统瘫痪了一周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 immobilize</td><td>/ɪˈmoʊbəlaɪz/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使不能动，固定住</td><td>immobilize the injured leg<br>固定受伤的腿</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Doctors immobilized his broken leg with a heavy plaster cast.  医生用厚石膏固定了他骨折的腿。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 incapacitate</td><td>/ˌɪnkəˈpæsɪteɪt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使失去能力，使瘫痪</td><td>incapacitate the whole system<br>使整个系统瘫痪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The virus incapacitated the hospital's computers for two full days.  病毒使医院的电脑瘫痪了整整两天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 prostrate</td><td>/ˈprɑːstreɪt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词、及物动词</td><td>平躺的，俯卧的；使极度虚弱</td><td>lie prostrate on the ground<br>平躺在地上</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The exhausted runner lay prostrate on the ground after crossing the line.  那名精疲力竭的跑者冲线后平躺在地上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hangdog</b></td><td>/ˈhæŋdɔːɡ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>伤心的，沮丧的</td><td>a hangdog expression<br>沮丧的表情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He came home with a hangdog look after failing the exam again.  又一次考试不及格后，他一脸沮丧地回了家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 crestfallen</td><td>/ˈkrestfɔːlən/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>垂头丧气的，沮丧的</td><td>look utterly crestfallen<br>显得十分沮丧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He came home crestfallen after losing the final match by one point.  决赛以一分之差落败后，他垂头丧气地回了家。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 dejected</td><td>/dɪˈdʒektɪd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>沮丧的，失望的</td><td>a dejected look<br>沮丧的神情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She left the interview dejected and sure she had failed.  她面试后神情沮丧，确信自己失败了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 despondent</td><td>/dɪˈspɑːndənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>沮丧的，绝望的</td><td>grow despondent about work<br>对工作变得沮丧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After three rejections he became despondent about ever publishing.  三次被拒后他对出版感到绝望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 disconsolate</td><td>/dɪsˈkɑnsələt/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>郁郁不乐的，忧伤的</td><td>a disconsolate look<br>忧伤的神情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The disconsolate boy sat alone after losing the final match.  输掉决赛后，郁郁不乐的男孩独自坐着。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 doleful</td><td>/ˈdoʊlfəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>悲哀的，忧郁的</td><td>a doleful voice<br>悲哀的语调</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She told the whole story in a low doleful voice.  她用低沉悲哀的语调讲完了整件事。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 melancholy</td><td>/ˈmelənkɑːli/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>忧郁，伤感</td><td>a mood of quiet melancholy<br>淡淡的忧郁情绪</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Autumn rain always fills him with a vague melancholy.  秋雨总让他心里泛起一种莫名的忧郁。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sorrowful</td><td>/ˈsɑːroʊfəl/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>悲伤的，忧伤的</td><td>a sorrowful farewell<br>悲伤的告别</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Her sorrowful eyes told us the news before she spoke.  她悲伤的眼睛在她开口前就告诉了我们消息。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 buoyant</td><td>/ˈbuːjənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>心情好的</td><td>in a buoyant mood<br>心情愉快</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He arrived in a buoyant mood after hearing the good news.  听到好消息后，他心情愉快地到了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 elated</td><td>/ɪˈleɪtəd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>兴高采烈的，得意的</td><td>elated by the news<br>因消息而狂喜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was elated when the university finally accepted her application.  大学终于录取她时，她欣喜若狂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 sprightly</td><td>/ˈspraɪtli/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>活泼的，充满活力的</td><td>a sprightly old man<br>一位活泼的老人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A sprightly old man led the hikers up the steep mountain path.  一位活泼的老人带着徒步者爬上陡峭的山路。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 blissful</td><td>/ˈblɪsfəl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>极幸福的，快乐的</td><td>a blissful silence<br>幸福的宁静</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They spent a blissful week beside the quiet mountain lake.  他们在宁静的山湖边度过了幸福的一周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 delighted</td><td>/dɪˈlaɪtəd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>高兴的，欣喜的</td><td>delighted with the gift<br>对礼物很欣喜</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She was delighted to hear about her friend's promotion.  听说朋友升职，她非常高兴。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 joyous</td><td>/ˈdʒɔɪəs/</td><td>★★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>快乐的，欢乐的</td><td>a joyous occasion<br>欢乐的场合</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The village held a joyous festival after the autumn harvest.  村里在秋收后办了欢乐的节庆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 jubilant</td><td>/ˈdʒuːbɪlənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>喜悦的</td><td>a jubilant crowd<br>欢欣的人群</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nominee gave a short speech before the jubilant crowd.  被提名人在欢欣鼓舞的人群前发表了简短讲话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 upbeat</td><td>/ˈʌpbiːt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>乐观向上的，令人振奋的</td><td>an upbeat mood<br>乐观的心情</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Despite the heavy loss, the coach stayed upbeat about the coming season.  尽管惨败，教练对下个赛季依然乐观。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>hanker</b></td><td>/ˈhæŋkər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>不及物动词</td><td>向往，渴望</td><td>hanker after fame<br>渴望名声</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After years in the city she hankered after the quiet countryside.  在城里待了多年后，她开始向往宁静的乡村。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 appetite</td><td>/ˈæpəˌtaɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>欲望，爱好；食欲</td><td>an appetite for risk<br>对风险的偏好</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The public has a huge appetite for stories about lost cities.  公众对失落之城的故事有极大兴趣。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 craving</td><td>/ˈkreɪvɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>强烈渴望，渴求</td><td>a craving for sweets<br>对甜食的渴望</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She had a sudden craving for chocolate late at night.  深夜她突然特别想吃巧克力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hunger</td><td>/ˈhʌŋɡər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>渴望；饥饿</td><td>a hunger for knowledge<br>对知识的渴望</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His hunger for knowledge kept him reading until dawn.  对知识的渴望让他读书到黎明。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 itch</td><td>/ˈɪtʃ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>不及物动词、名词</td><td>渴望；发痒</td><td>itch to travel<br>渴望去旅行</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The children were itching to open their presents early.  孩子们急着想早点拆礼物。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 longing</td><td>/ˈlɔːŋɪŋ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>渴望，思念；渴望的</td><td>a longing for home<br>对家的思念</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A deep longing for home filled her every single winter.  每个冬天她都充满对家的深切思念。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 lust</td><td>/ˈlʌst/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>强烈欲望，渴望</td><td>a lust for power<br>对权力的强烈欲望</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His lust for power destroyed several old and valuable friendships.  他对权力的强烈欲望毁掉了几段珍贵的旧友情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 passion</td><td>/ˈpæʃən/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>名词</td><td>热情，激情</td><td>a passion for music<br>对音乐的热爱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He talks about old trains with genuine passion.  他谈起老火车时充满真挚的热情。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 thirst</td><td>/ˈθɜːrst/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、不及物动词</td><td>渴望；口渴</td><td>a thirst for knowledge<br>求知欲</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He thirsted for recognition far more than for money.  他对认可的渴望远超对金钱的渴望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 yearning</td><td>/ˈjɜːrnɪŋ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>名词、形容词</td><td>渴望，向往</td><td>a yearning for home<br>对家的渴望</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She felt a deep yearning for the mountains of her childhood.  她对童年的群山怀有深深的渴望。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 odium</td><td>/ˈoʊdiəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>憎恶，讨厌</td><td>public odium<br>公众的憎恶</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Public odium followed the executives long after the company had been dissolved.  公司解散很久之后，公众的憎恶仍伴随着这些高管。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 lack of desire</td><td>/læk əv dɪˈzaɪr/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>名词</td><td>缺乏欲望，没有兴致</td><td>a lack of desire<br>缺乏欲望</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His lack of desire to travel surprised all of his friends.  他对旅行毫无兴致，这让朋友们都很吃惊。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>haphazard</b></td><td>/hæpˈhæzərd/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>随意的；杂乱无章的</td><td>a haphazard manner<br>杂乱无章的方式</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The files were stacked in a haphazard pile on the office floor.  文件杂乱无章地堆在办公室的地板上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 aimless</td><td>/ˈeɪmləs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>无目的的，无方向的</td><td>an aimless life<br>漫无目的的生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 After graduation he drifted through several aimless years in the city.  毕业后他在城里虚度了几年漫无目的的生活。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 arbitrary</td><td>/ˈɑːrbətreri/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>专横的，独断专行的；随意的</td><td>an arbitrary decision<br>武断的决定</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The rules seemed completely arbitrary to the students.  这些规定在学生看来完全是随意的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 desultory</td><td>/ˈdesəlˌtɔːri/</td><td>★</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>散漫的，无条理的</td><td>desultory conversation<br>散漫的谈话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 They made desultory notes instead of a real research plan.  他们做了些散漫的笔记，而非真正的研究计划。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 erratic</td><td>/ɪˈrætɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>反复无常的，不规律的</td><td>erratic driving behavior<br>飘忽不定的驾驶行为</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His erratic driving behavior alarmed the passengers long before the police stopped him.  早在警察拦下他之前，他飘忽不定的驾驶行为就让乘客惊慌。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 scattered</td><td>/ˈskætərd/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>分散的，零星的</td><td>scattered showers<br>零星阵雨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Scattered books and papers covered every surface of his small desk.  零散的书和纸铺满了他小书桌的每一处。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stray</td><td>/streɪ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>漫无目的的</td><td>stray thoughts<br>零散的念头</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A few stray thoughts kept interrupting her careful reading of the contract.  几个零散的念头不断打断她对合同的细读。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 methodical</td><td>/məˈθɑːdɪkl/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>井然有序的，有条理的</td><td>a methodical approach<br>有条理的方法</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A methodical search of the archive finally produced the missing land records.  对档案的有条理搜查终于找出了缺失的土地记录。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 systematic</td><td>/ˌsɪstəˈmætɪk/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>系统的，有条理的</td><td>a systematic review<br>系统性回顾</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The team took a systematic approach to checking each component.  团队用系统的方法逐一检查了每个部件。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 nonrandom</del></td><td>/ˌnɑnˈrændəm/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>非随机的，有规律的</td><td>a nonrandom pattern<br>非随机的模式</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The nonrandom spacing of the trees suggested careful human planning.  树木非随机的间距说明这是人为精心规划的。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 orderly</td><td>/ˈɔːrdərli/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有秩序的，整齐的</td><td>an orderly queue<br>整齐的队伍</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The students left the hall in an orderly line.  学生们排成整齐的队伍离开礼堂。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 organized</td><td>/ˈɔːrɡəˌnaɪzd/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>有条理的，有组织的</td><td>highly organized files<br>条理分明的文件</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She keeps an organized notebook for every course she takes.  她为每门课都备有一本条理分明的笔记。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 regular</td><td>/ˈreɡjələr/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>规律的，通常的</td><td>on a regular basis<br>定期地</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He goes swimming on a regular basis every week.  他每周都定期去游泳。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Unit 10

<table>
<thead><tr>
<th>单词</th>
<th>音标</th>
<th>常用</th>
<th>口语</th>
<th>词性</th>
<th>中文释义</th>
<th>常用短语</th>
</tr></thead>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>harangue</b></td><td>/həˈræŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词、名词</td><td>（发表）长篇大论</td><td>a long harangue<br>长篇的训话</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The manager harangued his staff for twenty minutes about missed deadlines.  经理就错过截止日期的事对员工发表了二十分钟的长篇训话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 diatribe</td><td>/ˈdaɪətraɪb/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>长篇抨击性演讲</td><td>launch into a diatribe<br>开始长篇抨击</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His speech turned into a long diatribe against modern popular music.  他的演讲变成了对现代流行音乐的长篇抨击。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 jeremiad</td><td>/ˌdʒerəˈmaɪəd/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>哀诉，悲叹式的抨击文</td><td>a gloomy jeremiad<br>阴郁的哀叹长文</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His column became a weekly jeremiad about modern manners.  他的专栏成了每周对现代风气的哀叹式抨击。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 philippic</del></td><td>/fɪˈlɪpɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>激烈的抨击演说</td><td>deliver a fierce philippic<br>发表激烈的抨击演说</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The senator's philippic against the bill lasted two full hours.  这位参议员抨击该法案的演说整整两小时。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rant</td><td>/rænt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>（尤指长时间的）训斥，责骂</td><td>go on a rant<br>大发一通牢骚</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The coach went on a long rant about the team's careless defending.  教练就球队漫不经心的防守训斥了很久。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 speak temperately</del></td><td>/spiːk ˈtɛmpərətli/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>动词</td><td>温和地讲话</td><td>speak temperately in public<br>公开场合温和发言</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Even when provoked, he chose to speak temperately to the angry crowd.  即使被激怒，他也选择对愤怒的人群温和地讲话。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>harass</b></td><td>/həˈræs/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>烦扰</td><td>harass the witness<br>骚扰证人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Enemy cavalry harassed the retreating army for three long days.  敌方骑兵对撤退的军队骚扰了漫长的三天。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>harbinger</b></td><td>/ˈhɑːrbɪndʒər/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>名词</td><td>预兆，先兆；预示者</td><td>a harbinger of spring<br>春天的先兆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The first robin is often seen as a harbinger of spring.  第一只知更鸟常被视为春天的预兆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 foregoer</del></td><td>/ˈfɔːrˌɡoʊər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>先行者，前辈</td><td>a foregoer of reform<br>改革的先行者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 This little machine was the foregoer of the modern computer.  这台小机器是现代计算机的先行者。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 herald</td><td>/ˈherəld/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>预示，预兆</td><td>herald a new era<br>预示新时代来临</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The invention heralded a new era in communication.  这项发明预示着通信新时代的到来。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 outrider</td><td>/ˈaʊtraɪdər/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>先驱，前导者</td><td>an outrider of change<br>变革的先驱</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The first robins are outriders of the coming spring.  第一批旅鸫是春天将至的先驱。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 precursor</td><td>/priːˈkɜːrsər/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>名词</td><td>先驱者，先导</td><td>a precursor to war<br>战争的先兆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Rising tensions along the border proved a precursor to open war.  边境紧张局势加剧，成了公开战争的先兆。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>harbor</b></td><td>/ˈhɑːrbər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>藏匿，庇护；心怀（怨恨）</td><td>harbor a deep grudge<br>心怀深深怨恨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The family was accused of harboring a fugitive for several months.  这家人被指控藏匿一名逃犯数月。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 bear</td><td>/ˈber/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>动词</td><td>承受，支撑；忍受</td><td>bear the weight<br>承受重量</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 These thin beams cannot bear the weight of the roof.  这些细梁承受不了屋顶的重量。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cherish</td><td>/ˈtʃerɪʃ/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>珍爱，珍视</td><td>cherish a memory<br>珍藏一段回忆</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 I will always cherish the years we spent together.  我会永远珍视我们共度的那些年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 entertain</td><td>/ˌentərˈteɪn/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使娱乐，招待；考虑</td><td>entertain the guests<br>招待客人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She entertained us with stories about her years abroad.  她讲述在国外的岁月，让我们听得很开心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hold</td><td>/ˈhoʊld/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>怀有，心存</td><td>hold a grudge<br>心怀怨恨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He still holds a grudge against his former business partner.  他仍然对昔日的生意伙伴心怀怨恨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 nurse</td><td>/ˈnɜːrs/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>培育，照料；护士</td><td>nurse a grudge<br>心怀怨恨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He nursed the small business through three very difficult years.  他呵护这家小企业度过了三个极艰难的年头。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del><b>hardbitten</b></del></td><td>/hɑːrdˈbɪtn/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>顽强的，经受得住困境、压力的</td><td>a hardbitten reporter<br>老练顽强的记者</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only a hardbitten reporter would keep digging after so many threats.  只有顽强的记者才会在受到如此多威胁后继续追查。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 hardened</td><td>/ˈhɑːrdənd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>变得坚强的；铁石心肠的</td><td>a hardened criminal<br>惯犯，老手</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Even hardened soldiers were shaken by what they saw there.  连久经沙场的老兵都被眼前所见震住了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 sturdy</td><td>/ˈstɜːrdi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>强健的，结实的</td><td>a sturdy wooden table<br>结实的木桌</td></tr>

</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 tough</td><td>/ˈtʌf/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>艰难的，费力的；坚韧的</td><td>a tough assignment<br>艰巨的任务</td></tr>

</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 cast-iron</td><td>/ˌkæst ˈaɪərn/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>坚强的，坚不可摧的</td><td>a cast-iron alibi<br>无懈可击的证词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He has a cast-iron stomach and can eat almost anything.  他有铁打的胃，几乎什么都能吃。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 inured</td><td>/ɪˈnjʊrd/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>形容词</td><td>习惯于艰苦的</td><td>inured to hardship<br>习惯了艰苦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The miners had grown inured to the cold and the dust.  矿工们已习惯了寒冷和粉尘。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 rugged</td><td>/ˈrʌɡəd/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>坚毅的；粗糙不平的</td><td>rugged terrain<br>险峻的地形</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Only rugged travelers attempt that trail in the winter.  只有意志坚毅的旅行者才会在冬天走那条小径。</td></tr>

<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 stout</td><td>/ˈstaʊt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>坚定的，勇敢的；粗壮的</td><td>stout resistance<br>顽强的抵抗</td></tr>

<tr><td>↳近 vigorous</td><td>/ˈvɪɡərəs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>精力旺盛的，强有力的</td><td>vigorous exercise<br>剧烈运动</td></tr>

<tr><td>↳近 hardy</td><td>/ˈhɑːrdi/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>顽强的</td><td>a hardy plant<br>耐寒的植物</td></tr>

<tr><td>↳反 delicate</td><td>/ˈdeləkət/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>微妙的，精细的；脆弱的</td><td>a delicate distinction<br>微妙的区别</td></tr>

<tr><td><del>↳反 nonhardy</del></td><td>/ˌnɑnˈhɑrdi/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不耐寒的，脆弱的</td><td>nonhardy garden plants<br>不耐寒的花草</td></tr>

<tr><td>↳反 soft</td><td>/ˈsɑːft/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>柔和的，轻微的</td><td>a soft voice<br>轻柔的声音</td></tr>

<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 balanced</td><td>/ˈbælənst/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>均衡的，协调的</td><td>a balanced diet<br>均衡的饮食</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The room felt calm because the colors were carefully balanced.  房间给人平静感，因为色彩经过精心搭配。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 congruous</td><td>/ˈkɑŋɡruəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>一致的，适合的，协调的</td><td>congruous with the setting<br>与环境协调</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The plain wooden benches were congruous with the simple chapel.  素木长凳与这座朴素的礼拜堂很协调。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 consonant</td><td>/ˈkɑːnsənənt/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>和谐一致的</td><td>consonant with tradition<br>与传统相符</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His modest lifestyle is consonant with the values he preaches.  他朴素的生活方式与他宣扬的价值观相符。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 eurythmic</del></td><td>/jʊˈrɪθmɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>和谐匀称的</td><td>eurythmic proportions<br>匀称的比例</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The temple has eurythmic columns that please the eye at once.  这座神庙匀称和谐的立柱令人一见舒心。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 harmonic</td><td>/hɑrˈmɑːnɪk/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>和谐的，和声的</td><td>harmonic balance<br>和谐的平衡</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The two voices created a rich harmonic blend.  两个声部形成了丰富和谐的融合。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 accordant</del></td><td>/əˈkɔrdənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>一致的，符合的</td><td>accordant with the law<br>与法律相符</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The ruling was accordant with decisions made in earlier cases.  该裁决与早先案件的判决一致。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 coherent</td><td>/koʊˈhɪrənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>连贯的，条理清晰的</td><td>a coherent argument<br>条理清晰的论证</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 He was too tired to give a coherent account of the accident.  他太累了，无法条理清晰地讲述事故。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 compatible</td><td>/kəmˈpætəbl/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>一致的，能共存的</td><td>compatible with the system<br>与该系统兼容</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The new driver is compatible with both older printers.  新驱动与两款旧打印机都兼容。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 concordant</td><td>/kənˈkɔrdənt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>一致的，和谐的</td><td>concordant testimony<br>一致的证词</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Both witnesses gave concordant accounts of that evening.  两位证人对那天晚上的叙述一致。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 conformable</td><td>/kənˈfɔrməbəl/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>顺从的；一致的</td><td>conformable to the rules<br>符合规定的</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The design must be conformable to the city's new safety code.  设计必须符合城市新的安全规范。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 congruent</td><td>/ˈkɑːŋɡruənt/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>和谐一致的；全等的</td><td>congruent with the facts<br>与事实相符</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His account is congruent with the facts we already know.  他的说法与我们已知的事实相符。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 correspondent</td><td>/ˌkɔːrəˈspɑːndənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词、名词</td><td>相符的，一致的；记者</td><td>correspondent to the facts<br>与事实相符</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His testimony was correspondent to the notes taken that night.  他的证词与那晚记下的笔记相符。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 nonconflicting</del></td><td>/ˌnɑnkənˈflɪktɪŋ/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不冲突的，相容的</td><td>nonconflicting schedules<br>不冲突的日程</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 We finally found two nonconflicting dates for the meeting.  我们终于找到两个不冲突的开会日期。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 disharmonic</del></td><td>/ˌdɪshɑrˈmɑnɪk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不和谐的，不协调的</td><td>a disharmonic chord<br>不和谐的和弦</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The composer opened the piece with a deliberately disharmonic chord.  作曲家用一个刻意不和谐的和弦开始这首曲子。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 disharmonious</del></td><td>/ˌdɪshɑrˈmoʊniəs/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不和谐的，不一致的</td><td>a disharmonious relationship<br>不和睦的关系</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Their disharmonious partnership collapsed within a single season.  他们不和的合作关系在一个赛季内就崩溃了。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 incongruous</td><td>/ɪnˈkɑːŋɡruəs/</td><td>★☆</td><td>☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不协调的，不相称的</td><td>an incongruous mixture<br>不协调的混合</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His loud tie looked incongruous with the somber gray suit.  他花哨的领带与深灰西装很不协调。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 unbalanced</td><td>/ʌnˈbælənst/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>形容词</td><td>不平衡的；失衡的</td><td>an unbalanced diet<br>不均衡的饮食</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The report gave an unbalanced picture of the city's economy.  这份报告对该市经济的描述有失均衡。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 inconsistent</td><td>/ˌɪnkənˈsɪstənt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★☆</td><td>形容词</td><td>不一致的，矛盾的</td><td>inconsistent with the facts<br>与事实不符</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 His testimony was inconsistent with the facts established by the police.  他的证词与警方查明的事实不符。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>harness</b></td><td>/ˈhɑːrnɪs/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>利用</td><td>harness solar energy<br>利用太阳能</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Engineers are learning to harness wind power in far colder climates.  工程师正在学习在更寒冷的气候中利用风力。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 apply</td><td>/əˈplaɪ/</td><td>★★★★</td><td>★★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>运用，应用</td><td>apply a rule<br>运用规则</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Try to apply what you learned to a real problem.  试着把学到的东西用到实际问题上。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 employ</td><td>/emˈplɔɪ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>雇用；使用</td><td>employ new staff<br>雇用新员工</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The factory employs nearly six hundred workers in this town.  这家工厂在本镇雇用了近六百名工人。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exercise</td><td>/ˈeksərˌsaɪz/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>运用，行使</td><td>exercise caution<br>谨慎行事</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Judges must exercise restraint when public feeling runs high.  当民情激昂时，法官必须保持克制。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 exploit</td><td>/ɪkˈsplɔɪt/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>最大程度地利用</td><td>exploit natural resources<br>开发自然资源</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The company plans to exploit the island's natural resources responsibly.  公司计划负责任地开发这个岛的自然资源。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 operate</td><td>/ˈɑːpəreɪt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、不及物动词</td><td>操作，运用</td><td>operate a machine<br>操作机器</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She learned to operate the crane in only two weeks.  她只用两周就学会了操作起重机。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 utilize</td><td>/ˈjuːtəˌlaɪz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★★☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>利用，使用</td><td>utilize local resources<br>利用当地资源</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The village utilizes wind power to pump water from the well.  这个村子利用风力从井里抽水。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 fail to utilize</td><td>/feɪl tə ˈjutəlaɪz/</td><td>★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>动词</td><td>未能利用</td><td>fail to utilize resources<br>未能利用资源</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Many firms fail to utilize the data they already collect.  许多公司未能利用自己已经收集的数据。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>harrow</b></td><td>/ˈhæroʊ/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>折磨，使苦恼</td><td>harrow his conscience<br>折磨他的良心</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The memory of that night harrowed him for the rest of his life.  那一夜的记忆折磨了他的余生。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 agonize</td><td>/ˈæɡənaɪz/</td><td>★</td><td>★★</td><td>动词</td><td>（使）非常痛苦，苦苦思索</td><td>agonize over a decision<br>为决定苦苦纠结</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 She agonized for weeks over whether to accept the job.  她为是否接受这份工作苦恼了好几周。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 beset</td><td>/bɪˈset/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>使苦恼，骚扰；攻击，袭击</td><td>beset by doubts<br>为疑虑所困</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The young author was beset by doubts about his second novel.  这位年轻作者被对第二部小说的疑虑所困扰。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 besiege</td><td>/bɪˈsiːdʒ/</td><td>★</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>围攻；不断烦扰</td><td>besiege the city<br>围攻城市</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Reporters besieged the minister with questions about the scandal.  记者们就丑闻不断向部长发问。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 torment</td><td>/tɔːrˈment/</td><td>★★</td><td>★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>折磨，使痛苦；痛苦</td><td>torment the prisoners<br>折磨囚犯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Guilt tormented him for years after the accident.  事故之后，负罪感折磨了他多年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 torture</td><td>/ˈtɔːrtʃər/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>及物动词、名词</td><td>折磨，拷打；酷刑</td><td>under torture<br>在酷刑之下</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The long wait for results was pure torture for her.  漫长等待结果对她来说简直是折磨。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳近 excruciate</del></td><td>/ɪkˈskruːʃieɪt/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>及物动词</td><td>折磨，使痛苦</td><td>excruciate the prisoners<br>折磨囚犯</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The interrogators excruciated the prisoners for information they never had.  审讯者折磨囚犯，逼问他们从未掌握的情报。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳近 plague</td><td>/pleɪɡ/</td><td>★★★</td><td>★★★</td><td>名词、动词</td><td>瘟疫；折磨，使…痛苦</td><td>plagued by doubts<br>被疑虑折磨</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Doubts about the decision plagued him for the rest of the year.  对那个决定的疑虑折磨了他余下的一整年。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 assuage</td><td>/əˈsweɪdʒ/</td><td>★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>缓和，减轻；平息</td><td>assuage his fears<br>减轻他的恐惧</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The mayor's speech did little to assuage public anger.  市长的演讲几乎没能平息公众的怒火。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><b>harry</b></td><td>/ˈhæri/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>不断烦扰，骚扰</td><td>harry the enemy<br>不断骚扰敌人</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 Small cavalry units harried the retreating army for three straight days.  小股骑兵连续三天不断骚扰撤退的军队。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 comfort</td><td>/ˈkʌmfərt/</td><td>★★★☆</td><td>★★★★★</td><td>名词、及物动词</td><td>舒适；安慰</td><td>live in comfort<br>过舒适的生活</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The old chair gave him a great deal of comfort.  那把旧椅子给他带来极大的舒适。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td>↳反 mollify</td><td>/ˈmɑːlɪfaɪ/</td><td>☆</td><td>★</td><td>及物动词</td><td>平息，抚慰，缓和</td><td>mollify an angry customer<br>安抚愤怒的顾客</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 A sincere apology from the manager mollified the furious client.  经理诚挚的道歉安抚了这位愤怒的客户。</td></tr>
</tbody>
<tbody>
<tr><td><del>↳反 Everyman’swork</del></td><td>/ˈɛvrimænz ˌwɜrk/</td><td>☆</td><td>☆</td><td>名词</td><td>每个人的作品（原书粘连）</td><td>Everyman’swork as printed<br>原书粘连的词条</td></tr>
<tr><td colspan="7">例 The string Everyman’swork is a run-on form of every man's work.  字符串 Everyman’swork 是 every man's work 粘连而成。</td></tr>
</tbody>
</table>
